    W_2\bigl(\operatorname{Law}(Z_h+\mathcal E_h),\bm\pi\bigr)
    \leqslant C\sqrt d\,(h^3+h^{7/2})
    \leqslant C\sqrt d\,h^3\,.
\]
Now, we want to replace the exact path in our analysis with the predictor. Compare the exact midpoint update \(Z_h+\mathcal E_h\) with
the implementable update \eqref{eq:EM-x}--\eqref{eq:EM-v}, using the
same initial state and Brownian path.  The predictor
\eqref{eq:uld-predictor} satisfies
\[
    X_{h/2}-X_{h/2}^{\mathrm{pred}}
    =-\int_0^{h/2}a_{h/2-s}\,
       \bigl\{\nabla V(X_s)-\nabla V(X_0)\bigr\}\,\mathrm d s\,.
\]
Since \(a_t\leqslant t\), \(\nabla V\) is \(1\)-Lipschitz, and
stationarity gives \(\lVert X_s-X_0\rVert_2\leqslant\sqrt d\,s\),
\[
    \lVert X_{h/2}-X_{h/2}^{\mathrm{pred}}\rVert_2
    \leqslant\sqrt d\int_0^{h/2}(h/2-s)\,s\,\mathrm d s
    =\frac{\sqrt d\,h^3}{48}\,.
\]
The force coefficients in the position and momentum updates are
\(h a_{h/2}\) and \(h\mathrm{e}^{-h}\), respectively.  This coupling therefore gives
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
    W_2\bigl(\bm\pi K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}},\operatorname{Law}(Z_h+\mathcal E_h)\bigr)
    &\leqslant h\sqrt{a_{h/2}^2+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}}\,
       \lVert X_{h/2}-X_{h/2}^{\mathrm{pred}}\rVert_2
    \leqslant C\sqrt d\,h^4\,.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
Finally, the triangle inequality and
\(\lVert T\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant C\), for the metric transformation
defined in \Cref{sec:underdamped-upper}, give
\[
    W_{2,M}(\bm\pi K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}},\bm\pi)
    \leqslant C\,\bigl\{
       W_2\bigl(\operatorname{Law}(Z_h+\mathcal E_h),\bm\pi\bigr)
       +\sqrt d\,h^4\bigr\}
    \leqslant C\sqrt d\,h^3\,.\qedhere
\]
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Use a synchronous coupling and put
\(\Delta x\coloneqq x-x'\), \(\Delta p\coloneqq p-p'\), and
\(\Delta z\coloneqq(\Delta x,\Delta p)\).
Let \(y,y'\) be the predictor positions
\eqref{eq:uld-predictor} from \((x,p)\) and \((x',p')\), using the
same Brownian path.  Define the Hessians averaged along the segments
joining the initial and predictor positions by
\[
\begin{aligned}
 H_{\mathrm{init}}&\coloneqq\int_0^1\nabla^2V(x'+s\Delta x)\,\mathrm d s\,,\\
 H_{\mathrm{pred}}&\coloneqq\int_0^1\nabla^2V(y'+s(y-y'))\,\mathrm d s\,.
\end{aligned}
\]
Each lies between \(\kappa^{-1}I_d\) and \(I_d\);
\(H_{\mathrm{pred}}\) may depend on the noise.
The predictor difference and the output differences satisfy exactly
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 g&\coloneqq H_{\mathrm{pred}}\,
       \{\Delta x+a_{h/2}\Delta p-c_{h/2}H_{\mathrm{init}}\, \Delta x\}\,,\\
 \Delta x^+&=\Delta x+a_h\Delta p-h a_{h/2}g\,,\qquad
 \Delta p^+=\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Delta p-h\mathrm{e}^{-h}g\,.
 \end{aligned}
\]
Since \(a_t=(1-\mathrm{e}^{-2t})/2\),
\begin{equation}
 \begin{split}
 \lVert (\Delta x^+,\Delta p^+)\rVert_M^2-\lVert \Delta z\rVert_M^2
 &=-\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-4h}}2\,\lVert \Delta p\rVert^2
   -2h\,\Bigl\langle\Delta x+\frac{1+\mathrm{e}^{-3h}}2\,\Delta p, g\Bigr\rangle
 \\
   &\qquad+\frac{h^2\,(1+\mathrm{e}^{-2h})}2\,\lVert g\rVert^2\,.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:uld-midpoint-exact-coupling-energy}
\end{equation}
The following quantity will be useful when computing the dissipation,
\[
 \mathcal D\coloneqq2\,\lVert \Delta p\rVert^2+
     2\,\langle \Delta x+\Delta p,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\,\Delta x\rangle\,.
\]
Use the normalized metric transformation
\(T(\Delta x,\Delta p)=(\Delta x,\Delta x+\Delta p)\), and set
\(\xi\coloneqq T\Delta z\), so that
\(\lVert \xi\rVert^2=\lVert \Delta z\rVert_M^2\).
The deterministic part of the update in the \(T\)-coordinates is
\[
 A\coloneqq T\begin{bmatrix}
       0&I_d\\
       -H_{\mathrm{pred}}&-2I_d
     \end{bmatrix}T^{-1}
   =\begin{bmatrix}
       -I_d&I_d\\
       I_d-H_{\mathrm{pred}}&-I_d
     \end{bmatrix}\,.
\]
Its symmetric part is
\[
 \frac{A+A^\top}{2}
 =\begin{bmatrix}
    -I_d&I_d-\frac12\,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\\
    I_d-\frac12\,H_{\mathrm{pred}}&-I_d
   \end{bmatrix}\,.
\]
For each eigenvalue \(\lambda\in[\kappa^{-1},1]\) of
\(H_{\mathrm{pred}}\), the corresponding two-dimensional block
has eigenvalues \(-1\pm(1-\lambda/2)\), namely
\(-\lambda/2\) and \(-2+\lambda/2\). Hence
\((A+A^\top)/2\preceq-I_{2d}/(2\kappa)\),
\[
 \mathcal D=-2\,\langle \xi,A\xi\rangle
 \geqslant\kappa^{-1}\,\lVert \xi\rVert^2
 =\kappa^{-1}\,\lVert \Delta z\rVert_M^2\,.
\]
We also have
\[
 \mathcal D\geqslant\lVert \Delta p\rVert^2+
           \langle \Delta x,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\,\Delta x\rangle\,.
\]
To see this, subtract its right-hand side from
\(\mathcal D\) and complete the square using
\((H_{\mathrm{pred}})^2\preceq
H_{\mathrm{pred}}\).

First substitute
\(\bar g\coloneqq H_{\mathrm{pred}}\,(\Delta x+a_{h/2}\Delta p)\)
for \(g\) in the right-hand side of
\eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-exact-coupling-energy}.
For brevity, write
\(a\coloneqq a_{h/2}\) and
\(b\coloneqq(1+\mathrm{e}^{-3h})/2\). Then
\[
 \langle \Delta x+b\Delta p,\bar g\rangle
 =\langle \Delta x,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta x\rangle
  +(a+b)\,\langle \Delta p,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta x\rangle
  +ab\,\langle \Delta p,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta p\rangle\,.
\]
Since \((1-\mathrm{e}^{-4h})/2=2h+O(h^2)\) and \(a+b=1+O(h)\), the
leading terms in \eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-exact-coupling-energy} give
\(-h\mathcal D\); the last term in the preceding display contributes
\(-2hab\,\langle \Delta p,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta p\rangle\leqslant0\).
The coefficient errors are controlled by the preceding lower bound on
\(\mathcal D\), since \(H_{\mathrm{pred}}^2\preceq H_{\mathrm{pred}}\) gives
\[
 \bigl|\langle \Delta p,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta x\rangle\bigr|
 \leqslant\frac12\bigl(\lVert \Delta p\rVert^2+\langle \Delta x,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta x\rangle\bigr)
 \leqslant\frac12\,\mathcal D\,.
\]
The positive term involving \(\lVert \bar g\rVert^2\) is also controlled by
\(\mathcal D\):
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \lVert \bar g\rVert^2
 &\leqslant2\,\lVert H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta x\rVert^2
       +2a^2\,\lVert H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta p\rVert^2\\
 &\leqslant2\,\langle \Delta x,H_{\mathrm{pred}}\Delta x\rangle
       +2a^2\,\lVert \Delta p\rVert^2
 \leqslant C\mathcal D\,.
\end{aligned}
\]

Thus, this substitution gives an upper bound
\(-h\mathcal D+Ch^2\mathcal D\).

The predictor correction satisfies
\[
 g-\bar g=-c_{h/2}H_{\mathrm{pred}}
                          H_{\mathrm{init}}\,\Delta x\,,\qquad
 \lVert g-\bar g\rVert\leqslant Ch^2\,\lVert \Delta x\rVert\,.
\]
We also have
\[
 \Bigl\lvert\langle \Delta x+\frac{1+\mathrm{e}^{-3h}}2\,\Delta p,g-\bar g\rangle\Bigr\rvert
 \leqslant Ch^2\sqrt{\mathcal D}\,\lVert \Delta x\rVert\,.
\]
Together with \(\lVert \bar g\rVert\leqslant C\sqrt{\mathcal D}\),
\eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-exact-coupling-energy} consequently yields
\[
 \begin{split}
 \lVert (\Delta x^+,\Delta p^+)\rVert_M^2-\lVert \Delta z\rVert_M^2
 &\leqslant-h\mathcal D+Ch^2\mathcal D
       +Ch^3\sqrt{\mathcal D}\,\lVert \Delta x\rVert+Ch^6\,\lVert \Delta x\rVert^2\\
 &\leqslant-\frac34\,h\mathcal D
       +Ch^5\,\lVert \Delta z\rVert_M^2\,.
 \end{split}
\]
Here the last line uses Young's inequality and a universal upper bound
on \(h\). If \(h\leqslant c\kappa^{-1/4}\), choose \(c\) small
enough to absorb \(Ch^5\) into \(h/(4\kappa)\). We obtain
\[
 \lVert (\Delta x^+,\Delta p^+)\rVert_M^2
 \leqslant\Bigl(1-\frac h{2\kappa}\Bigr)\,
                      \lVert \Delta z\rVert_M^2\,.
\]
This holds for every common noise realization. Integrating over an
optimal initial coupling and taking square roots gives the stated
contraction factor \(1-h/(4\kappa)\).
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rem:uld-midpoint-sharpness}
The range \(h\leqslant c\kappa^{-1/4}\) cannot be improved in general:
when the averaged Hessians at the initial and predictor positions do
not commute, the exponential midpoint kernel can expand \(W_{2,M}\) by
a term of order \(h^5\).  Choose a smooth convex function
\(\psi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}\) such that \(0\leqslant\psi''\leqslant1/4\), with
\(\psi''=1/4\) near zero and \(\psi''=0\) on \([2,\infty)\).  For
\(0<\alpha\leqslant1/4\), let
\[
 V_\alpha(x)\coloneqq\frac\alpha2\,\lVert x\rVert^2+\frac{x_2^2}{8}
                   +\psi(x_1+x_2)\,,\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^2\,.
\]
Then, \(\alphaI_2\preceq\nabla^2V_\alpha\preceqI_2\), and
\(\nabla^2V_\alpha\) is constant on the strip near \(x_1+x_2=0\) where
\(\psi''=1/4\), and on the half-plane \(x_1+x_2\geqslant2\).  Let
\(e_1\) be the first coordinate vector, and let
\[
 \Delta x\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}
  1\\[0.4em]
  \displaystyle\frac{c_{h/2}}{8\,\{1-c_{h/2}\,(\alpha+1/2)\}}
 \end{bmatrix}\,.
\]
Start the synchronous coupling at \((0,L e_1)\) and
\((\varepsilon_0\,\Delta x,L e_1)\), where \(\varepsilon_0>0\) is small
enough that the segment joining the initial positions lies in the strip and $L$ is a constant.
For either initial position
\(x\in\{0,\varepsilon_0\Delta x\}\), the predictor
\eqref{eq:uld-predictor} is
\[
 X_{h/2}^{\mathrm{pred}}(x)
 =x+a_{h/2}L e_1-c_{h/2}\nabla V_\alpha(x)+\eta\,,
\]
where \(\eta\) is the common Gaussian noise, whose law does not depend
on \(L\). In particular, the predictor's coordinate sum satisfies
\[
 \sum_{i=1}^2\bigl(X_{h/2}^{\mathrm{pred}}(x)\bigr)_i
 =a_{h/2}L+\sum_{i=1}^2
       \bigl(x_i-c_{h/2}\partial_iV_\alpha(x)+\eta_i\bigr)\,.
\]
For fixed \(h>0\), we have \(a_{h/2}>0\), while the remaining terms
do not depend on \(L\). Thus both predictors lie in the half-plane
\(\{y\in\mathbb{R}^2:y_1+y_2\geqslant2\}\) with probability tending to one
as \(L\to\infty\). By convexity of this half-plane, the segment
joining them also lies there.
On this event, the averaged Hessians are
\[
 H_{\mathrm{init}}
 =\begin{bmatrix}\alpha+1/4&1/4\\1/4&\alpha+1/2\end{bmatrix}\,,\qquad
 H_{\mathrm{pred}}
 =\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&0\\0&\alpha+1/4\end{bmatrix}\,,
\]
and, since the momentum difference vanishes, the output difference is
\(\varepsilon_0\,(\Delta x-h a_{h/2}g,-h\mathrm{e}^{-h}g)\) with
\(g\coloneqq H_{\mathrm{pred}}\,
(I_2-c_{h/2}H_{\mathrm{init}})\,\Delta x\).
The gradient is \(1\)-Lipschitz, so dominated convergence shows that
the difference of the output means tends to this vector.

For \(0<h\leqslant1\), the second component of
\((I_2-c_{h/2}H_{\mathrm{init}})\,\Delta x\) is
\(-c_{h/2}/8\), and its first component lies in \((0,1]\).
Using \eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-exact-coupling-energy} with \(\Delta p=0\)
and \(c_{h/2}=(h-1+\mathrm{e}^{-h})/4\geqslant h^2/12\) gives
\[
 \begin{split}
 &\lVert (\Delta x-h a_{h/2}g,-h\mathrm{e}^{-h}g)\rVert_M^2-\lVert (\Delta x,0)\rVert_M^2\\
 &\qquad=-2h\,\langle \Delta x,g\rangle+
              \frac{h^2\,(1+\mathrm{e}^{-2h})}2\,\lVert g\rVert^2
 \geqslant-2\alpha h+\frac{h c_{h/2}^2}{128}
 \geqslant-2\alpha h+\frac{h^5}{18432}\,.
 \end{split}
\]
The \(W_{2,M}\) distance between the output distributions is at least
the \(M\)-norm of the difference of their means.  Hence, if
\(\alpha<h^4/36864\), it exceeds the initial distance
\(\varepsilon_0\,\lVert (\Delta x,0)\rVert_M\) for sufficiently large finite
\(L\).  Taking \(\alpha=\kappa^{-1}\) shows that a uniform contraction
range must satisfy \(h\lesssim\kappa^{-1/4}\).
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
We first return to the original parameters
\(\alpha,\beta\) and \(\gamma=2\sqrt\beta\).  Let
\[
    \overline V(y)\coloneqq V(y/\sqrt\beta)\,,
    \qquad
    \mathsf S_\beta(x,p)\coloneqq(\sqrt\beta\,x,p)\,.
\]
Write \(\overline K_s^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\) for the exponential midpoint
kernel with potential \(\overline V\), friction \(2\), and step
\(s\).  Let \(\overline{\bm\pi}\) be the phase space target
associated with \(\overline V\).
The update formulas \eqref{eq:EM-x}--\eqref{eq:EM-v} give
\[
    \mathsf S_{\beta\#}(\mu K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})
    =(\mathsf S_{\beta\#}\mu)\,\overline K_{\sqrt\beta\,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\,,
    \qquad
    \mathsf S_{\beta\#}\bm\pi=\overline{\bm\pi}\,.
\]
The definition of \(M_\gamma\) also gives
\[
    \lVert \mathsf S_\beta z\rVert_{M}
    =\sqrt\beta\,\lVert z\rVert_{M_\gamma}\,.
\]
For \(0<\sqrt\beta\,h\leqslant{c\kappa^{-1/4}}\), with the universal
constant \(c\) chosen so that \(c\leqslant h_0\),
\Cref{prop:uld-midpoint-numerical-contraction,prop:uld-midpoint-one-step}
therefore give, for all \(\mu,\nu\in\mathcal P_2(\mathbb{R}^{2d})\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
    W_{2,M_\gamma}(\mu K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}},\nu K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})
    &\leqslant\Bigl(1-\frac{\sqrt\beta\,h}{4\kappa}\Bigr)\,
       W_{2,M_\gamma}(\mu,\nu)\,,\\
    \sqrt\beta\,W_{2,M_\gamma}
       (\bm\pi K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}},\bm\pi)
    &\leqslant C\sqrt d\,(\sqrt\beta\,h)^3\,.
\end{aligned}
\]

Since \(\nabla V\) is globally Lipschitz, the update formulas show
that \(K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\) preserves finite second moments.
Together with the contraction bound, completeness of
\(\mathcal P_2(\mathbb{R}^{2d})\) under \(W_{2,M_\gamma}\)
\cite[Proposition~1.3.15]{ChewiBook} allows us to apply Banach's fixed
point theorem.  This gives a unique invariant distribution
\(\bm\pi_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\in\mathcal P_2(\mathbb{R}^{2d})\).
Dividing the stationary one step error by the contraction gap gives
\[
    \sqrt\beta\,W_{2,M_\gamma}
       (\bm\pi_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}},\bm\pi)
    \leqslant C\kappa\sqrt d\,(\sqrt\beta\,h)^2\,.
\]

Position projection is \(1\)-Lipschitz in the
\(M_\gamma\)-norm.  Multiplying the preceding bound by
\(\sqrt\alpha/\sqrt\beta=\kappa^{-1/2}\) proves
\eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-position-bias}.

For \(\widehat X_0=x_\star\) and
\(\widehat P_0\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\), strong convexity and
integration by parts give
\(\mathbb{E}_\pi\lVert X-x_\star\rVert^2\leqslant d/\alpha\).
Couple the initial momentum identically with the independent Gaussian
momentum under \(\bm\pi\). The definition of \(M_\gamma\) then bounds the
initial squared distance by
\[
    \beta\,W_{2,M_\gamma}
       (\delta_{x_\star}\otimes\mathsf{N}(0,I_d),\bm\pi)^2
    \leqslant2\beta\,\mathbb{E}_\pi\lVert X-x_\star\rVert^2
    \leqslant2\kappa d\,.
\]
Iterating the rescaled contraction and stationary error bounds against
\(\bm\pi\) yields
\[
    \sqrt\beta\,W_{2,M_\gamma}
       \bigl((\delta_{x_\star}\otimes\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))
                    (K_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})^n,\bm\pi\bigr)
    \leqslant C\sqrt{\kappa d}\,\mathrm{e}^{-cn\sqrt\beta\,h/\kappa}
       +C\kappa\sqrt d\,(\sqrt\beta\,h)^2\,.
\]
After projection to position and multiplication by
\(\sqrt\alpha/\sqrt\beta=\kappa^{-1/2}\), this proves
\eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-finite-time-explicit}.
To reach the accuracy criterion \eqref{eq:global-absolute-criterion},
choose
\[
    \sqrt\beta\,h={c\kappa^{-1/4}d^{-1/4}\varepsilon^{1/2}}\,, \qquad
    n=\Bigl\lceil
       C\,\frac\kappa{\sqrt\beta\,h}\,
       \log\Bigl(\mathrm{e}+\frac d{\varepsilon^2}\Bigr)
      \Bigr\rceil\,,
\]
with \(c\) sufficiently small and \(C\) sufficiently large.
Since \(\varepsilon\leqslant\sqrt d/2\), this step lies in the
range \(\sqrt\beta\,h\leqslant c\kappa^{-1/4}\).
The step choice makes the stationary term in
\eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-finite-time-explicit} at most
\(\varepsilon/2\); the iteration count makes the transient term at
most \(\varepsilon/2\).
Since
\[
    \frac\kappa{\sqrt\beta\,h}
    {=\frac1c\,\kappa^{5/4}d^{1/4}\varepsilon^{-1/2}}\,,
\]
and since \(d/\varepsilon^2\geqslant4\) gives
\(\log(\mathrm{e}+d/\varepsilon^2)\leqslant2\log(d/\varepsilon^2)\),
the stated choice of \(n\) has the order claimed in
\eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-complexity}.
Each step evaluates the gradient at the current point and at the
predictor, so the query count is at most \(2n\).  Absorbing this
factor of two into the universal constant proves
\eqref{eq:uld-midpoint-complexity}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:et-one-step-defect}
Fix a numerical \(0<a_\star<1/4\), and let
\[
 V(x)\coloneqq\frac{x^2}{2}+a_\star h\cos(x/\sqrt h)\,,\qquad
 \pi\propto\mathrm{e}^{-V}\,,\qquad f(x)\coloneqq\sqrt h\cos(x/\sqrt h)\,.
\]
For all sufficiently small \(h>0\), the Heun kernel \(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}\)
has a unique invariant distribution \(\mu\) with finite second moment, and
\[
 W_2(\mu,\pi)\geqslant
 \frac12\,\bigl\lvert\pi(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}f-f)\bigr\rvert
 \geqslant ch^{3/2}\,.
\]
The constants are numerical once \(a_\star\) is fixed.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
For fixed \(g=\sqrt{2h}\,G\), put \(E_g(x)\coloneqq x-hV'(x)+g\).
The Heun update is \(\{x+E_g(E_g(x))\}/2\). Since
\(V''\in[1-a_\star,1+a_\star]\), for \(h\,(1+a_\star)\leqslant1\)
its Lipschitz constant is at most
\(\{1+(1-h\,(1-a_\star))^2\}/2<1\).
Also, $K_h^{\rm Heun}(0,\cdot)$ has finite second moment, so contraction gives the
unique invariant distribution in \(\mathcal P_2\). In particular,
\(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}f\) is \(1\)-Lipschitz. Invariance therefore gives
\[
 \lvert\pi(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}f-f)\rvert
 =\lvert(\pi-\mu)(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}f-f)\rvert\leqslant2W_2(\mu,\pi)\,.
\]

Let \(Z,G\) be independent standard Gaussians and define
\begin{equation*}
 A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}\coloneqq1-h+\frac{h^2}{2}\,,\qquad
 \sigma_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}\coloneqq\sqrt{2h}\,\Bigl(1-\frac h2\Bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Write
\[
 \theta\coloneqq Z/\sqrt h\,,\qquad
 U\coloneqq(A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}Z+\sigma_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}G)/\sqrt h\,,\qquad
 W\coloneqq((1-h)\,Z+\sqrt{2h}\,G)/\sqrt h\,.
\]
The exact output of Heun started at $Z$, divided by \(\sqrt h\), is
\[
 U+\frac{a_\star h}{2}\,
       \{(1-h)\sin\theta+\sin(W+a_\star h\sin\theta)\}\,.
\]
Since
\[
 \lvert\sin(W+a_\star h\sin\theta)-\sin W\rvert\leqslant a_\star h\,,
 \qquad
 \bigl\lvert\mathrm{e}^{-a_\star h\cos\theta}-1+a_\star h\cos\theta\bigr\rvert
       \leqslant Ca_\star^2h^2\,,
\]
the target normalizing constant satisfies
\[
 \mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{-a_\star h\cos\theta}
    =1-a_\star h\mathrm{e}^{-1/(2h)}+O(h^2)=1+O(h^2)\,.
\]
The Gaussian variances are
\[
 \begin{split}
 \operatorname{Var}(U-\theta)
   &=(A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}-1)^2/h+2\,(1-h/2)^2=2+O(h)\,,\\
 \operatorname{Var}(U-W)&=h^3/4+h^2/2\,,\\
 \operatorname{Var}(U+\theta)&\geqslant(A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}+1)^2/h\geqslant c/h\,,\\
 \operatorname{Var}(U+W)&\geqslant(A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}+1-h)^2/h\geqslant c/h\,.
 \end{split}
\]
Thus, the Gaussian characteristic function and the sine and cosine
product identities give
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathbb{E}[\cos U\cos\theta]&=\tfrac12\,\mathrm{e}^{-1}+O(h)\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}[\sin U\sin\theta]=\tfrac12\,\mathrm{e}^{-1}+O(h)\,,\\
 \mathbb{E}[\sin U\sin W]&=\tfrac12+O(h)\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\cos U\,,\ \mathbb{E}\cos\theta=O(\mathrm{e}^{-c/h})\,.
 \end{split}
\]
For \(\varphi(x)\coloneqq\cos(x/\sqrt h)\), Taylor's inequality
\(\lvert\cos(s+t)-\cos s+t\sin s\rvert\leqslant t^2/2\) yields
\[
 \begin{split}
 (1+O(h^2))\,\pi(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}\varphi)
  &=\mathbb{E}\cos U-a_\star h\,\mathbb{E}[\cos U\cos\theta]\\
   &\qquad -\frac{a_\star h}{2}\,
     \{(1-h)\,\mathbb{E}[\sin U\sin\theta]+\mathbb{E}[\sin U\sin W]\}+O(h^2)\\
     &=-\frac{a_\star h}{4}\,(1+3\mathrm{e}^{-1})+O(h^2)\,,\\
 (1+O(h^2))\,\pi(\varphi)
 &=\mathbb{E}\cos\theta-a_\star h\,\mathbb{E}\cos^2\theta+O(h^2)
   =-\frac{a_\star h}{2}+O(h^2)\,.
 \end{split}
\]
Thus,
\(\pi(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}f-f)
 =-a_\star\,(3\mathrm{e}^{-1}-1)\,h^{3/2}/4+O(h^{5/2})\).
The leading coefficient is non-zero, proving the claim.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- proposition-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:heun-gaussian-lower}
For every \(d\geqslant1\) and \(0<h\leqslant h_0\), Heun on
\(V=\lVert \cdot\rVert^2/2\) has a unique invariant distribution with finite second
moment, and
\begin{equation}
    W_2(\widehat\pi_h^{\mathrm{Heun}},\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))
       {\asymp\sqrt d\,h^2}\,.
    \label{eq:et-gaussian-lower}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-11 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
For the Heun method, one step is
\[
    X^+=\Bigl(1-h+\frac{h^2}{2}\Bigr)\,X
       +\sqrt{2h}\,\Bigl(1-\frac h2\Bigr)\,G_d\,,
\]
where \(G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\) is independent of \(X\).
Its invariant distribution is $\mathsf{N}(0,s_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}I_d)$, where
\begin{equation*}
    s_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}
    \coloneqq\frac{2h-2h^2+h^3/2}
       {2h-2h^2+h^3-h^4/4}
    =1-\frac{h^2}{4}+O(h^3)\,.
\end{equation*}
The exact formula
$W_2(\mathsf{N}(0,sI_d),\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))=\sqrt d\,\lvert\sqrt s-1\rvert$
gives \eqref{eq:et-gaussian-lower}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Let \(\pi^{(1)},K,\mu\) be the scalar target, Heun kernel, and invariant
distribution in \Cref{lem:et-one-step-defect}, and put
\(\varphi(x)\coloneqq\cos(x/\sqrt h)\). The scalar calculation gives
\[
 c h\leqslant\lvert\pi^{(1)}(K\varphi-\varphi)\rvert\,,
 \qquad \operatorname{Lip}(\sqrt h\varphi)\leqslant1\,,
 \qquad \operatorname{Lip}(K(\sqrt h\varphi))\leqslant1\,.
\]
The Lipschitz expectation bound and \(\mu K=\mu\) imply
\[
 W_2(\pi^{(1)}K,\pi^{(1)})\geqslant ch^{3/2}\,,\qquad
 2W_2(\mu,\pi^{(1)})\geqslant ch^{3/2}\,.
\]
For any bounded \(g\), Pinsker's inequality gives
\( \lvert(\nu-\pi^{(1)})(g)\rvert
 \leqslant\lVert g\rVert_\infty\sqrt{2\operatorname{KL}(\nu\mathrel{\Vert}\pi^{(1)})}\).
Apply this with \((\nu,g)=(\pi^{(1)}K,\varphi)\) and
\((\mu,K\varphi-\varphi)\), using
\(\lVert \varphi\rVert_\infty\leqslant1\),
\(\lVert K\varphi-\varphi\rVert_\infty\leqslant2\), and invariance. Then
\[
 \min\{\operatorname{KL}(\pi^{(1)}K\mathrel{\Vert}\pi^{(1)}),\,
        \operatorname{KL}(\mu\mathrel{\Vert}\pi^{(1)})\}\geqslant ch^2\,.
\]
The target and both numerical laws are products. Additivity of squared
\(W_2\) and KL over products, summed over the \(d-1\geqslant d/2\)
oscillatory coordinates, proves \eqref{eq:overdamped-common-ET} and the KL bound.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:stationary-affine-perturbation}
Let \((U_n,G_n,\xi_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) be jointly stationary, with
\(U_n,\xi_n\in\mathbb{R}^p\), \(\mathbb{E}\lVert U_0\rVert^2<\infty\), and independent
\(G_n\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_p)\). Suppose
\[
 U_{n+1}=\Lambda U_n+\Sigma G_n+\xi_n\,,\qquad
 \Lambda\coloneqq\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_p)\,,\quad
 \Sigma\coloneqq\operatorname{diag}(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_p)\,,
\]
where \(0\leqslant\lambda_i<1\) and \(\lvert\xi_{n,i}\rvert\leqslant b_i\)
almost surely for \(1\leqslant i\leqslant p\).
Then,
for every \(1\leqslant i\leqslant p\),
\[
 \lVert U_{0,i}\rVert_{L^2}\leqslant
 \frac{\lvert\sigma_i\rvert}{\sqrt{1-\lambda_i^2}}+\frac{b_i}{1-\lambda_i}\,.
\]
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
For every integer \(N\geqslant1\), iteration gives
\[
 U_0=\Lambda^NU_{-N}
       +\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Lambda^j\Sigma G_{-1-j}
       +\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Lambda^j\xi_{-1-j}\,.
\]
For \(1\leqslant i\leqslant p\), stationarity and independence give $\lVert \lambda_i^NU_{-N,i}\rVert_{L^2}
   =\lambda_i^N\,\lVert U_{0,i}\rVert_{L^2}\to 0$.
Also, \(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\lambda_i^j\,\lvert\xi_{-1-j,i}\rvert
\leqslant b_i/(1-\lambda_i)\) almost surely.
The asserted bounds follow from the triangle inequality, by letting $N\to \infty$.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:heun-modulated-block}
There are numerical \(a,c,C,h_0>0\), \(\tau>1\), and \(K_0\geqslant2\)
such that,
for every integer \(m\geqslant1\), \(K\geqslant K_0\), and
\(0<h\leqslant h_0\) with \(mh\leqslant1\), the potential with
\(\ell^2=h/\tau\),
\begin{equation}
 V(x,y)\coloneqq\frac{\lVert x\rVert^2}{2}+\frac{y^2}{2K}
       +\ell^2\mathcal A(y/\sqrt K)\sum_{i=1}^m\cos(x_i/\ell)\,,
 \qquad \mathcal A(t)\coloneqq a\,(2+\sin t)\,,
 \label{eq:heun-modulated-potential}
\end{equation}
satisfies
\((1-5a)D\preceq\nabla^2V\preceq(1+5a)D\), where
\(D\coloneqq\operatorname{diag}(I_m,K^{-1})\).
Its Heun invariant distribution \(\mu\in\mathcal P_2\) and target
\(\pi\propto\mathrm{e}^{-V}\) satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\lvert\mathbb{E}_\mu Y-\mathbb{E}_\pi Y\rvert}{\sqrt K}\geqslant cmh^2\,,
 \qquad
 \mathbb{E}_\mu\bigl[\lVert X\rVert^2+Y^2/K\bigr]\leqslant Cm\,.
 \label{eq:heun-modulated-bias}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Fix \(0<a\leqslant1/100\); we choose \(\tau\) large and then
\(h_0\leqslant1/\tau\) small. Constants \(C\) below are independent of
\(\tau,m,K,h\). Since \(m\ell^2\leqslant1/\tau\leqslant1\),
\[
 \lVert D^{-1/2}\nabla^2V D^{-1/2}-I_{m+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
 \leqslant3a+a\sqrt{m\ell^2}+am\ell^2\leqslant5a\,.
\]
Thus, \Cref{prop:et-contractivity} gives \(\mu\in\mathcal P_2(\mathbb{R}^{m+1})\).
Put \(\theta_j\coloneqq X_j/\ell\) for \(j\in[m]\), \(\eta\coloneqq Y/\sqrt K\),
\(r\coloneqq h/K\).
We use the Heun coefficients
\(A_t^{\mathrm{Heun}}\coloneqq1-t+t^2/2\) and
\(\sigma_t^{\mathrm{Heun}}\coloneqq\sqrt{2t}\,(1-t/2)\).
For fresh independent standard
Gaussians \(G_0,\ldots,G_m\), the Heun predictors, for every \(j\in[m]\), are

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \theta_j^{\mathrm{pred}}
 &=\underbrace{(1-h)\,\theta_j+\sqrt{2\tau}\,G_j}_{\bar\theta_j^{\mathrm{pred}}}
   +h\mathcal A(\eta)\sin\theta_j\,,\\
 \eta^{\mathrm{pred}}
 &=\underbrace{(1-r)\,\eta+\sqrt{2r}\,G_0}_{\bar\eta^{\mathrm{pred}}}
   -r\ell^2\mathcal A'(\eta)\sum_{k\in[m]}\cos\theta_k\,.
 \end{aligned}
\]
The corrected outputs are
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \theta_j^+
 &=\underbrace{A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}\theta_j+\frac{\sigma_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}}{\ell}\,G_j}_{\bar\theta_j}
   +\underbrace{\frac h2\,\Bigl\{(1-h)\,\mathcal A(\eta)\sin\theta_j
             +\mathcal A(\eta^{\mathrm{pred}})\sin\theta_j^{\mathrm{pred}}\Bigr\}}_{\zeta_j}\,,\\
 \eta^+
 &=\underbrace{A_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}\eta+\sigma_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}\,G_0}_{\bar\eta}
   -{\underbrace{\frac{r\ell^2}{2}\,\Bigl\{(1-r)\,\mathcal A'(\eta)
          \sum_{k\in[m]}\cos\theta_k
          +\mathcal A'(\eta^{\mathrm{pred}})
          \sum_{k\in[m]}\cos\theta_k^{\mathrm{pred}}\Bigr\}}_{\zeta_0}}\,.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:heun-modulated-update}
\end{equation}
Since \(\lvert\mathcal A\rvert\leqslant3a\) and
\(\lvert\mathcal A'\rvert\leqslant a\), these perturbations satisfy
\(\lvert\zeta_j\rvert\leqslant3ah\) for \(j\in[m]\) and
\(\lvert\zeta_0\rvert\leqslant am\ell^2r\).
To apply \Cref{lem:stationary-affine-perturbation}, take \(p=m+1\) and the
stationary vector \((\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_m,\eta)\).
The perturbation vector in that lemma is
\((\zeta_1,\ldots,\zeta_m,-\zeta_0)\): the weak coordinate is indexed
by \(m+1\) in the lemma and by \(0\) in the block update.
Its first \(m\) affine
coefficients are \(A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}\), its last is \(A_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}\), and the corresponding
Gaussian coefficients are \(\sigma_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}/\ell\) and
\(\sigma_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}\). The perturbation bounds are \(b_j=3ah\)
for \(j\in[m]\) and \(b_{m+1}=am\ell^2r\). Here,
\(1-A_t^{\mathrm{Heun}}=t\,(1-t/2)\) and
\(s_t^{\mathrm{Heun}}\coloneqq(\sigma_t^{\mathrm{Heun}})^2/(1-(A_t^{\mathrm{Heun}})^2)=(2-t)/(2-t+t^2/2)\leqslant1\).
Thus, for every \(j\in[m]\), the lemma gives
\[
 \lVert \theta_j\rVert_{L^2(\mu)}
 \leqslant\frac{\sqrt{s_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}}}{\ell}+\frac{3a}{1-h/2}
 \leqslant C\sqrt{\tau/h}\,,\qquad
 \lVert \eta\rVert_{L^2(\mu)}
 \leqslant\sqrt{s_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}}+\frac{am\ell^2}{1-r/2}
 \leqslant1+\frac{2a}{\tau}\,.
\]
Consequently, \(\mathbb{E}_\mu[\lVert X\rVert^2+Y^2/K]\leqslant Cm\).
In particular, for \(w(t)\coloneqq\mathcal A(t)\mathcal A'(t)=a^2\,(2\cos t+\tfrac12\sin(2t))\),
since
\(2\cos t+\tfrac12\,\sin(2t)\geqslant\tfrac32-t^2\),
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb{E}_\mu w(\eta)
 \geqslant a^2\,\Bigl\{\frac32-\mathbb{E}_\mu\eta^2\Bigr\}
 \geqslant a^2\,\Bigl\{\frac32-(1+2a/\tau)^2\Bigr\}
 \geqslant\frac{a^2}{4}\,.
 \label{eq:heun-block-positive}
\end{equation}

By permutation symmetry, we fix coordinate \(1\) for the scalar
calculations below.

\emph{Gaussian damping.}
For \(\chi(u,v)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_\mu\mathrm{e}^{\boldsymbol i(u\theta_1+v\eta)}\), stationarity gives
\[
 \lvert\chi(u,v)\rvert\leqslant
 \mathrm{e}^{-\tau(1-h/2)^2u^2-r(1-r/2)^2v^2}\,\lvert\chi(A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}}u,A_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}v)\rvert
       +3ah\,\lvert u\rvert+am\ell^2r\,\lvert v\rvert\,.
\]
If \(1/2\leqslant\lvert u\rvert\leqslant2\), \(\lvert v\rvert\leqslant2\), and
\(J\coloneqq\lfloor1/(8h)\rfloor\), then \(\lvert (A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}})^ju\rvert\geqslant1/4\) for
\(0\leqslant j\leqslant J\). Iteration yields, for small enough \(h\),
\begin{equation}
 \lvert\chi(u,v)\rvert\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-c\tau/h}
       +Ca h\sum_{j=0}^{J-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\tau j/32}\leqslant Ca h\,.
 \label{eq:heun-block-fourier}
\end{equation}
Define
\(C_\mu\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_\mu[\mathcal A'(\eta)\cos\theta_1]\) and
\(C_{\mathrm{pred}}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\mathcal A'(\eta^{\mathrm{pred}})
                                      \cos\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}]\).
Then, Gaussian integration and \eqref{eq:heun-block-fourier} give
\begin{equation}
 \begin{split}
 \lvert\mathbb{E}[\mathcal A'(\bar\eta)\cos\bar\theta_1]\rvert
 &\leqslant a\mathrm{e}^{-\tau(1-h/2)^2}
                  \max_{v\in\{-A_r^{\mathrm{Heun}},A_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}\}}\lvert\chi(A_h^{\mathrm{Heun}},v)\rvert
       \leqslant Ca^2h\mathrm{e}^{-\tau/2}\,,\\
 \lvert C_{\mathrm{pred}}\rvert
 &\leqslant a\mathrm{e}^{-\tau-r}\,\Bigl\{
       \max_{v\in\{r-1,1-r\}}\lvert\chi(1-h,v)\rvert
                  +3ah+am\ell^2r\Bigr\}
       \leqslant Ca^2h\mathrm{e}^{-\tau}\,.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:heun-block-damped-terms}
\end{equation}
For the second line, the two predictor components have independent
Gaussian noises conditional on the input; their phase perturbations
are bounded by \(3ah\) and \(am\ell^2r\).

\emph{The surviving term.}
Equation \eqref{eq:heun-modulated-update} implies
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathbb{E}\lvert\eta^{\mathrm{pred}}-\eta\rvert+\mathbb{E}\lvert\bar\eta-\eta\rvert&\leqslant C\sqrt r\,,\\
 \mathbb{E}\bigl\lvert\zeta_1-\tfrac h2\,\mathcal A(\eta)\,
             \{(1-h)\sin\theta_1+\sin\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}\}\bigr\rvert
 &\leqslant\tfrac{ah}{2}\,\mathbb{E}\lvert\eta^{\mathrm{pred}}-\eta\rvert
             +\tfrac{9a^2h^2}{2}\,.
 \end{split}
\]
Expanding the output cosine and using stationarity gives
\begin{align*}
    C_\mu &=\mathbb{E}[\mathcal A'(\bar\eta)\cos\bar\theta_1]
       -\tfrac h2\,\mathbb{E}[w(\eta)\sin\bar\theta_1\,
                    \{(1-h)\sin\theta_1+\sin\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}\}]+R\,, \\
 \lvert R\rvert
 &\leqslant Ca^2\,(m\ell^2r+h\sqrt r+ah^2)\,.
 \end{align*}

Replace \(\bar\theta_1\) by
\(\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}\) in the second term in the preceding Taylor expansion of
\(C_\mu\). Indeed,
\[
 \bar\theta_1-\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}
 =\frac{h^2}{2}\,\theta_1-\frac h2\,\sqrt{2\tau}\,G_1\,,
 \qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert\bar\theta_1-\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}\rvert
 \leqslant C\sqrt\tau\,h\,.
\]
Thus this replacement costs at most
\(Ca^2\sqrt\tau\,h^2\leqslant Ca^2h^{3/2}\).
Conditional on the input, \(\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}\) is Gaussian
with variance \(2\tau\).
Consequently,
\[
 \begin{split}
 \bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}[w(\eta)\sin\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}\sin\theta_1]\bigr\rvert
 &\leqslant3a^2\mathrm{e}^{-\tau}\,,\\
 \mathbb{E}[w(\eta)\sin^2\bar\theta_1^{\mathrm{pred}}]
 &=\frac12\,\mathbb{E}_\mu w(\eta)
   -\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-4\tau}}2\,
       \mathbb{E}[w(\eta)\cos(2(1-h)\theta_1)]\,.
 \end{split}
\]
Combining these two estimates with the Taylor expansion and
\eqref{eq:heun-block-damped-terms}, \(mh\leqslant1\), and
\(\tau h\leqslant1\) proves
\begin{equation}
 \bigl\lvert C_\mu+\tfrac h4\,\mathbb{E}_\mu w(\eta)\bigr\rvert
 \leqslant Ca^2h\,\Bigl\{\mathrm{e}^{-\tau/2}+\frac1{K\tau}
                  +\sqrt h\Bigr\}\,.
 \label{eq:heun-block-response}
\end{equation}
\emph{Comparison of means.}
Let \(C_\pi\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_\pi[\mathcal A'(\eta)\cos\theta_1]\).
The conditional target density relative to \(Z\sim\mathsf{N}(0,1)\) satisfies
\[
 \Bigl\lvert\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\ell^2\mathcal A(\eta)\cos(Z/\ell)}}
                  {\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{-\ell^2\mathcal A(\eta)\cos(Z/\ell)}}-1\Bigr\rvert
 \leqslant\mathrm{e}^{6a\ell^2}-1\leqslant Ca\ell^2\,,
 \qquad \lvert C_\pi\rvert\leqslant a\mathrm{e}^{-1/(2\ell^2)}+Ca^2\ell^2
                 \leqslant Ca^2h/\tau\,.
\]
Consequently \eqref{eq:heun-block-damped-terms} and
\eqref{eq:heun-block-response} imply
\[
 \frac{(1-r)\,C_\mu+C_{\mathrm{pred}}}{2-r}-C_\pi
 =-\tfrac h8\,\mathbb{E}_\mu w(\eta)
      +O\bigl(a^2h\,\{\mathrm{e}^{-\tau/2}+\tau^{-1}
                         +\sqrt h\}\bigr)\,.
\]
Choose the fixed numerical \(\tau\) so large, and then \(h_0\) so small,
that the error is at most \(a^2h/64\).
By \eqref{eq:heun-block-positive}, the displayed difference is at most
\(-a^2h/64\). Take expectations in
\(\eta^+=\bar\eta-\zeta_0\) from \eqref{eq:heun-modulated-update}
under stationarity. Since \(\mathbb{E}_\mu\eta^+=\mathbb{E}_\mu\eta\),
\(\mathbb{E}\bar\eta=A_r^{\mathrm{Heun}}\,\mathbb{E}_\mu\eta\), and, by permutation symmetry,
\(\mathbb{E}\zeta_0=\frac{r\ell^2m}{2}\,\{(1-r)\,C_\mu+C_{\mathrm{pred}}\}\),
we obtain
\(\mathbb{E}_\mu\eta=-m\ell^2\,\{(1-r)\,C_\mu+C_{\mathrm{pred}}\}/(2-r)\).
Integrating \(\partial_yV\) against the target gives
\(\mathbb{E}_\pi\eta=-m\ell^2C_\pi\). Together these give
\[
 \mathbb{E}_\mu\eta-\mathbb{E}_\pi\eta
   =-m\ell^2\,\Bigl\{\frac{(1-r)\,C_\mu+C_{\mathrm{pred}}}{2-r}-C_\pi\Bigr\}
   \geqslant\frac{a^2}{64\tau}\,mh^2\,.
\]
This proves the mean error in \eqref{eq:heun-modulated-bias}.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
\emph{Products of blocks.}

Reserve one coordinate with potential \(z^2/(2K)\). Set
\[
 m\coloneqq\min\{\lfloor(d-1)/2\rfloor,\lfloor h^{-1}\rfloor\}\,,\qquad
 q\coloneqq\lfloor(d-1)/(m+1)\rfloor\,,
\]
Let \(r\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1-q(m+1)}\) denote the quadratic padding
coordinates and \(z\in\mathbb{R}\) the reserved coordinate. With
\(x^{(j)}\in\mathbb{R}^m\), \(y_j\in\mathbb{R}\), and \(\ell^2=h/\tau\), the full
normalized potential is explicitly
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 V_h\bigl((x^{(j)},y_j)_{j=1}^q,r,z\bigr)
 &\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^q\Bigl\{
       \frac{\lVert x^{(j)}\rVert^2}{2}+\frac{y_j^2}{2K}\\
 &\qquad\qquad+a\ell^2\bigl(2+\sin(y_j/\sqrt K)\bigr)
          \sum_{i=1}^m\cos(x_i^{(j)}/\ell)\Bigr\}
       +\frac{\lVert r\rVert^2}{2}+\frac{z^2}{2K}\,.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:heun-product-potential}
\end{equation}
Thus it consists of \(q\) independent blocks from
\eqref{eq:heun-modulated-potential}, the padding coordinates, and the
reserved coordinate. The full potential satisfies
\((1-5a)I_d/K\preceq\nabla^2V_h\preceq(1+5a)I_d\).
Tensorization of the invariant distribution follows from independence
of the coordinate blocks and uniqueness. Its vector of \(y\)-coordinate
means and
\eqref{eq:heun-modulated-bias} imply
\[
 K^{-1/2}\,W_2(\widehat\pi_h^{\mathrm{Heun}},\pi_h)
 \geqslant c\sqrt q\,mh^2
 \asymp\min\{dh^2,\sqrt d\,h^{3/2}\}
 \asymp\frac{dh^2}{\sqrt{1+dh}}\,.
\]
Indeed, \(m\asymp\min\{d,h^{-1}\}\) and \(q\asymp d/m\).

\emph{Initialization and relaxation.}
Initialize all but the reserved coordinate at their numerical invariant
distributions, and set \(Z_0\coloneqq\sqrt K\). For each coupled block,
\eqref{eq:heun-modulated-bias} gives
\[
 \mathbb{E}_\mu[V(X,Y)-\min V]
 \leqslant\tfrac12\,\mathbb{E}_\mu[\lVert X\rVert^2+Y^2/K]+6am\ell^2\leqslant {Cm}\,.
\]
Thus, with \(d-1-q\,(m+1)\) quadratic padding coordinates,
\[
 \mathbb{E}[V_h(X_0)-V_h(x_\star)]
 \leqslant Cq\,(m+1)+C\,\{d-1-q\,(m+1)\}+\tfrac12\leqslant Cd\,.
\]
The block means remain stationary, while the reserved mean is
\(\sqrt K (A_{h/K}^{\mathrm{Heun}})^N\). Consequently, by projection onto these coordinates,
\[
 K^{-1/2}\,W_2(\nu_h(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}})^N,\pi_h)
 \geqslant c\,\frac{dh^2}{\sqrt{1+dh}}\,,\qquad
 K^{-1/2}\,W_2(\nu_h(K_h^{\mathrm{Heun}})^N,\pi_h)
 \geqslant (A_{h/K}^{\mathrm{Heun}})^N\,.
\]

\emph{Original units and the step restriction.}
For prescribed \(\alpha,\beta\) with \(\beta/\alpha=\kappa\), take
\(K=\kappa\,(1-5a)/(1+5a)\) and replace each constructed potential
\(V\) by \(x\mapsto V(\sqrt{\beta/(1+5a)}\,x)\).
Thus the block parameter \(K\) is comparable to the prescribed condition number \(\kappa\).
Increase \(\kappa_0\) so that \(K\geqslant K_0\).
The Hessian bounds become \(\alphaI_d,\betaI_d\), the step used
in the construction becomes \(\beta h/(1+5a)\), and the normalized
Wasserstein distance becomes
\(\sqrt{1-5a}\,K^{-1/2}\,W_2\). The expected energy is unchanged.
Since \(-\log A_t^{\mathrm{Heun}}\asymp t\) for small \(t>0\), sufficiently small
normalized accuracy \(\varepsilon\) therefore requires
\[
 \frac{d\,(\beta h)^2}{\sqrt{1+d\beta h}}\leqslant C\varepsilon\,,
 \qquad
 N\geqslant c\,\frac\kappa{\beta h}\,\log\frac1\varepsilon\,.
\]
Splitting the first inequality at \(d\beta h=1\) gives
\[
 \beta h\leqslant C\,\bigl\{
      (\varepsilon/d)^{1/2}+(\varepsilon^2/d)^{1/3}\bigr\}\,.
\]
The two terms balance at \(\varepsilon=d^{-1}\). Combining this bound
with the second inequality and the assumed small-step bound proves
\eqref{eq:linear-kappa-endpoint-rate}, hence also
\eqref{eq:linear-kappa-third-root}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
We normalize \(\beta=1\) and \(\gamma=2\), and consider both
unit curvature and the anisotropic target with \(\alpha=\kappa^{-1}\).
We first compute a variance expansion uniform
over the curvature \(\lambda\in(0,1]\). We then show that the variance
error persists at every iteration, while a displaced coordinate of
curvature \(\kappa^{-1}\) forces the required elapsed time.

\emph{Uniform scalar covariance expansion.}
For the potential \(\lambda x^2/2\), the state matrix is
\[
 M_{h,\lambda}\coloneqq
 \begin{bmatrix}
  1-h a_{h/2}\lambda\,(1-c_{h/2}\lambda)
       &a_h-h a_{h/2}^2\lambda\\[0.4em]
  -h\mathrm{e}^{-h}\lambda\,(1-c_{h/2}\lambda)
       &\mathrm{e}^{-2h}-h\mathrm{e}^{-h}a_{h/2}\lambda
 \end{bmatrix}\,.
\]
Its additive Gaussian noise, using the common Brownian path in
\eqref{eq:quadratic-flow-noise}, is
\[
 \eta_h-h\lambda\,
    \begin{bmatrix}a_{h/2}\\[0.25em]\mathrm{e}^{-h}\end{bmatrix}
       \, (\eta_{h/2})_x\,.
\]
Denote its noise covariance by \(Q_{h,\lambda}\), its
invariant position--momentum covariance by \(C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\),
and its scalar position variance by
\(s_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\coloneqq(C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{11}\).
The invariant covariance equation is
\[
 C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}
 =M_{h,\lambda}C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}M_{h,\lambda}^\top
     +Q_{h,\lambda}\,.
\]
At \(\lambda=1\), write \(C_h\coloneqq C_{h,1}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\) and
\(M_h\coloneqq M_{h,1}\), so this specializes to
\begin{equation}
 C_h=M_hC_hM_h^\top+Q_h\,.
 \label{eq:exponential-midpoint-Lyapunov}
\end{equation}
To control the remainder uniformly as \(\lambda\downarrow0\), write
the three covariance equations in the unknowns
\(\lambda(C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{11}\),
\((C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{12}\), and \((C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{22}\), and divide
each equation by \(h\). The coefficient matrix is analytic in
\((h,\lambda)\), including at \(h=\lambda=0\), and at \(h=0\) it is
\[
 \begin{bmatrix}
  0&-2&0\\1&2&-1\\0&2\lambda&4
 \end{bmatrix}\,.
\]
Its determinant is \(8\), independently of \(\lambda\).
The right-hand side is analytic as well. Hence these three unknowns
are analytic on a uniform neighborhood of
\(\{0\}\times[0,1]\). At \(\lambda=0\), their values are exactly
\[
 1\,,\qquad
 \frac{a_h-h\mathrm{e}^{-h}}{2a_h}\,,\qquad 1\,,
\]
with the middle expression extended continuously to zero at \(h=0\).
Thus \(\lambda(C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{11}-1\) and
\((C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{22}-1\) both have a factor \(\lambda\).
Taylor expansion of the covariance equations now gives
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 (C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{11}&=\lambda^{-1}-\frac{h^2}{24}+O(h^3)\,,\\
 (C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{12}&=\frac{h^2}{12}+O(h^3)\,,\\
 (C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{22}&=1+\frac{\lambda h^2}{12}+O(\lambda h^3)\,.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:em-uniform-curvature-covariance}
\end{equation}
All remainders are uniform over \(0<\lambda\leqslant1\).
In particular, \(C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\) is positive definite for a universal
range \(0<h\leqslant h_0\). The noise covariance is also positive
definite: the unmodified noise on the second half of the step already
has this property. The covariance equation therefore implies that
every eigenvalue of \(M_{h,\lambda}\) has modulus less than one.
This proves existence and uniqueness of the Gaussian invariant
distribution throughout this universal step size range.
Indeed, stability represents the stationary state as a convergent
sum of independent Gaussian noise variables, with covariance \(C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\).

For independent coordinates of curvatures
\(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_d\in(0,1]\), the Gaussian Wasserstein
formula and \eqref{eq:em-uniform-curvature-covariance} give
\[
 W_2\bigl((\bm\pi_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_x,\pi\bigr)
 =\frac{h^2}{48}\,\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^d\lambda_i\Bigr)^{1/2}
       \,\{1+O(h)\}\,.
\]
With all curvatures equal to one, this proves
\(s_h\coloneqq s_{h,1}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}=(C_h)_{11}
=1-h^2/24+O(h^3)\) and the isotropic lower bound
\eqref{eq:exponential-midpoint-lower}.
Taking \(\lambda_1=\cdots=\lambda_{d-1}=1\) and
\(\lambda_d=\kappa^{-1}\) proves the claimed stationary bias in
normalized units.

\emph{The variance error persists at every iteration.}
The initialization in the proposition gives each coordinate pair
covariance \(\operatorname{diag}(0,1)\). Equation
\eqref{eq:em-uniform-curvature-covariance} implies, uniformly in
\(\lambda\),
\[
 \det\bigl(C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-\operatorname{diag}(0,1)\bigr)
 =\frac{h^2}{12}+O(h^3)>0\,,\qquad
 C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-\operatorname{diag}(0,1)\succeq0\,.
\]
Consequently, the covariance after \(N\) updates is
\[
 C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}
   -M_{h,\lambda}^N\,
       \{C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-\operatorname{diag}(0,1)\}
          (M_{h,\lambda}^N)^\top
 \preceq C_{h,\lambda}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\,.
\]
Each position variance is therefore smaller than its target variance,
with a deficit at least as large as at stationarity. Applying the
Gaussian Wasserstein formula to the first \(d-1\geqslant d/2\)
coordinates yields
\[
 \kappa^{-1/2}\,W_2\bigl(\operatorname{Law}(\widehat X_N),\pi\bigr)
 \geqslant c\sqrt{\frac d\kappa}\,h^2\,.
\]

\emph{Relaxation of the displaced coordinate.}
For \(0<\lambda\leqslant1/2\) and \(h\leqslant h_0\), the two
eigenvalues of \(M_{h,\lambda}\), denoted by
\(\rho_+\) and \(\rho_-\), satisfy uniformly
\[
 0<\rho_-<\rho_+<1\,,\qquad
 1-\rho_+\asymp\lambda h\,,\qquad
 (M_{h,\lambda})_{11}\geqslant\rho_+\,.
\]
Indeed, the discriminant of the characteristic polynomial is
\(4\,(1-\lambda)h^2+O(h^3)\), and
\[
 (1-\rho_+)(1-\rho_-)
 =\det(I_2-M_{h,\lambda})
 =h a_h\lambda\,(1-c_{h/2}\lambda)\,.
\]
These formulas give \(1-\rho_-\asymp h\) and
\(1-\rho_+\asymp\lambda h\). The final inequality follows from
\((M_{h,\lambda})_{11}>(M_{h,\lambda})_{22}\) and
\((M_{h,\lambda})_{12}(M_{h,\lambda})_{21}<0\).
Since the initial momentum has expectation zero,
\[
 \begin{split}
 \frac{\mathbb{E}\widehat X_{N,d}}{\mathbb{E} X_{0,d}}
 &=\frac{(M_{h,\lambda})_{11}-\rho_-}{\rho_+-\rho_-}\,
            \rho_+^N
   +\frac{\rho_+-(M_{h,\lambda})_{11}}{\rho_+-\rho_-}\,
            \rho_-^N\\
 &\geqslant\rho_+^N\geqslant\mathrm{e}^{-CN\lambda h}\,,
 \qquad \lambda=\kappa^{-1}\,.
 \end{split}
\]
The initial position in this coordinate is \(\sqrt\kappa\).
The lower bound on Wasserstein distance by the distance of means
therefore supplies the second term in
\eqref{eq:em-anisotropic-finite-time}.

\emph{Original units and iteration count.}
The normalization in \Cref{sec:normalization} replaces the step by
\(\sqrt\beta\,h\) and the position distance by
\(\sqrt\beta\,W_2\). Thus multiplying normalized position distances
by \(\kappa^{-1/2}\) gives the accuracy
\(\sqrt\alpha\,W_2\) in the proposition, proving its stationary
formula and \eqref{eq:em-anisotropic-finite-time}.
Accuracy \(\varepsilon\leqslant1/2\) requires both
\[
 \sqrt\beta\,h
 \leqslant C\kappa^{1/4}d^{-1/4}\varepsilon^{1/2}\,,\qquad
 N\sqrt\beta\,h\geqslant c\kappa\log\frac1\varepsilon\,.
\]
Combining these bounds with \(\sqrt\beta\,h\leqslant h_0\)
proves \eqref{eq:em-anisotropic-iteration}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:scalar-linear-mixture}
Let \(X^+\coloneqq mX+\sqrt q\,G_d\), where \(q\geqslant0\), the pair
\((m,q)\) is fresh, and \(G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\) is independent of
\((X,m,q)\). If \(\mathbb{E} m^8<1\) and \(\mathbb{E} q^4<\infty\), the unique
invariant distribution in \(\mathcal P_2(\mathbb{R}^d) \) is
\(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt s\,G_d)\), with \(s\geqslant0\) independent of \(G_d\), and
\[
 s\stackrel{\mathrm d}=m^2s'+q\,,\qquad s'\perp(m,q)\,,\qquad s'\stackrel{\mathrm d}=s\,.
\]
The moments satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \begin{split}
 \overline s\coloneqq\mathbb{E} s&=\frac{\mathbb{E} q}{1-\mathbb{E} m^2}\,,\qquad
 \operatorname{Var}(s)=\frac{\operatorname{Var}(m^2\,\overline s+q)}{1-\mathbb{E} m^4}\,, \qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s-\overline s\rvert^4\leqslant\frac{C\,\mathbb{E}\lvert m^2\,\overline s+q-\overline s\rvert^4}{(1-\sqrt{\mathbb{E} m^8})^2}\,.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:linear-mixture-scalar-moments}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem:random-linear-mixture}
Consider \(Z_i^+\coloneqq MZ_i+Q^{1/2}G_i\), \(1\leqslant i\leqslant d\),
with a fresh common pair \((M,Q)\), \(Q\succeq0\), and independent
\(G_i\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_p)\), independent of the pair and the input.
Suppose \(\mathbb{E}\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^8<1\) and
\(\mathbb{E}\lVert Q\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^4<\infty\), allowing a fixed linear change
of state coordinates before checking these conditions.
Under the unique invariant distribution in
\(\mathcal P_2((\mathbb{R}^p)^d)\), the random vectors admit the joint decomposition
\((Z_i)_{i=1}^d\stackrel{\mathrm d}=(S^{1/2}G_i)_{i=1}^d\),
where the \(G_i\) are independent standard
Gaussians, independent of the random matrix \(S\succeq0\). Thus,
conditional on \(S\), their joint distribution is
\(\mathsf{N}(0,S)^{\otimes d}\), and
\begin{equation}
 S\stackrel{\mathrm d}=MS'M^\top+Q\,,\qquad
 S'\perp(M,Q)\,,\qquad
 \overline S\coloneqq\mathbb{E} S=\mathbb{E}[M\overline S M^\top+Q]\,.
 \label{eq:linear-mixture-fixed-point}
\end{equation}
Here \(S'\stackrel{\mathrm d}=S\). For a symmetric \(p\times p\)
matrix \(T\), write \({\operatorname{vech}(T)}\) for the column vector of its upper triangular
entries, listed row by row.
This convention retains each off-diagonal entry once, without
rescaling it; in particular,
\(\operatorname{vech}(T)=(T_{11},T_{12},T_{22})^\top\) when \(p=2\). Let \(L\) be the random matrix that represents the linear transformation
\(T\mapsto MTM^\top\) on these vectors, determined by
\[
 L\operatorname{vech}(T)=\operatorname{vech}(MTM^\top)\qquad\text{for every symmetric matrix }T\,.
\]
Define the vector
\[
 \mathfrak d\coloneqq \operatorname{vech}(M\overline S M^\top+Q-\overline S)\,.
\]
Write
\(\Gamma\coloneqq\operatorname{Cov}(\operatorname{vech}(S))\) for the covariance
of the random covariance coordinates. Then, the covariance and
centered fourth moment satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \Gamma=\mathbb{E}[L\Gamma\,L^\top]+\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak d\mathfrak d^\top]\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}{\lVert \operatorname{vech}(S-\overline S)\rVert}^4\leqslant\frac{C\,\mathbb{E}\lVert \mathfrak d\rVert^4}{\bigl(1-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^8}\bigr)^2}\,.
 \label{eq:linear-mixture-centered-moments}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Put \(\rho\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^8\).
For independent copies \((M_j,Q_j)_{j\geqslant0}\), put
\(P_0\coloneqqI\), \(P_j\coloneqq M_0\cdots M_{j-1}\). The series
\[
 Z_i\coloneqq\sum_{j=0}^\infty P_jQ_j^{1/2}G_{i,j}\,,\qquad
 S\coloneqq\sum_{j=0}^\infty P_jQ_jP_j^\top
\]
converge in \(L^8\) and \(L^4\), since
\(\mathbb{E}\lVert P_j\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^8\leqslant\rho^j\).
Conditioning on the coefficient pairs gives the mixture and
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-fixed-point}; synchronous coupling contracts
squared distances by \(\mathbb{E}\lVert M\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^2<1\).
Put \(R\coloneqq\operatorname{vech}(S-\overline S)\). Centering gives \(R\stackrel{\mathrm d}=LR'+\mathfrak d\), with
\(R'\perp(L,\mathfrak d)\) and \(\mathbb{E} R'=\mathbb{E}\mathfrak d=0\).
Taking expectations and covariances proves the moment identities in
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-scalar-moments} and
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-centered-moments}.

For the fourth moment, use the Hilbert--Schmidt norm
in the contracting state coordinates. Write \(R=\sum_{j=0}^\infty D_j\),
where \(D_j\coloneqq L_0\cdots L_{j-1}\mathfrak d_j\) are martingale differences satisfying
\(\lVert D_j\rVert_{L^4}\leqslant\rho^{j/4}\,\lVert \mathfrak d\rVert_{L^4}\).
For a partial sum \(U\) and its next difference \(D\), expansion gives,
with \(u\coloneqq\lVert U\rVert_{L^4}\), \(v\coloneqq\lVert D\rVert_{L^4}\),
\[
 \mathbb{E}\lVert U+D\rVert^4\leqslant u^4+6u^2v^2+4uv^3+v^4
 \leqslant(u^2+4v^2)^2\,.
\]
Thus,
\(\lVert R\rVert_{L^4}^2\leqslant4\sum_{j=0}^\infty\lVert D_j\rVert_{L^4}^2
\leqslant4\,\lVert \mathfrak d\rVert_{L^4}^2/(1-\sqrt\rho)\).
Equivalence of norms proves
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-centered-moments}.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem:gaussian-scale-perturbation}
Let \(X\) have a scale-Gaussian decomposition
\(X\stackrel{\mathrm d}=\sqrt s\,G_d\), where \(s\geqslant0\) is independent
of \(G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\), with
\(v_{\sqrt s}\coloneqq\operatorname{Var}(\sqrt s)<\infty\). Then,
\[
 W_2(\operatorname{Law}(X),\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))\geqslant c\min\{d v_{\sqrt s},\sqrt{d v_{\sqrt s}}\}\,.
\]
In particular, if
\[
 v_s\coloneqq\operatorname{Var}(s)\in(0,1]\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s-\mathbb{E} s\rvert^4\leqslant C_4 v_s^2\,,
\]
then, with \(c_{C_4}>0\) depending only on \(C_4\),
\begin{equation}
 W_2(\operatorname{Law}(X),\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))
 \geqslant c_{C_4}\min\{d v_s,\sqrt{d v_s}\}\,.
 \label{eq:mixture-variance-transfer}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Put \(\rho_d\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\lVert G_d\rVert\), \(v_d\coloneqq\operatorname{Var}(\lVert G_d\rVert)\),
\(b\coloneqq\mathbb{E} \sqrt s\), and \(\delta\coloneqqW_2(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt s\,G_d),\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))\).
Gaussian Poincar\'e gives \(v_d\leqslant1\)
\cite[Theorem~2.2.9]{ChewiBook}; hence, \(\rho_d^2=d-v_d\geqslant d-1\)
for \(d\geqslant2\), while \(\rho_1^2=2/\uppi\).
The norm is \(1\)-Lipschitz, so the differences of radial means and
standard deviations give
\[
 \rho_d\,\lvert b-1\rvert\leqslant\delta\,,\qquad
 \sqrt{d v_{\sqrt s}+b^2 v_d}\leqslant\sqrt{v_d}+\delta\,.
\]
Since \(b^2\geqslant2b-1\geqslant1-2\delta/\rho_d\),
\[
 d v_{\sqrt s}\leqslant\delta^2+2\,(\sqrt{v_d}+v_d/\rho_d)\,\delta
 \leqslant\delta^2+C\delta\,,
\]
which proves the first assertion.

For the second, let \(\overline s\coloneqq\mathbb{E} s\) and let \(s'\) be an independent copy.
H\"older and Cauchy--Schwarz give
\[
 \begin{split}
 (\mathbb{E}\lvert s-s'\rvert)^2
 &\geqslant\frac{(\mathbb{E}\lvert s-s'\rvert^2)^3}{\mathbb{E}\lvert s-s'\rvert^4}
 =\frac{8v_s^3}{2\,\mathbb{E}\lvert s-\overline s\rvert^4+6v_s^2}\geqslant\frac{4v_s}{C_4+3}\,,\\
 (\mathbb{E}\lvert s-s'\rvert)^2
 &\leqslant\mathbb{E}(\sqrt s-\sqrt{s'})^2\,
                \mathbb{E}(\sqrt s+\sqrt{s'})^2
 \leqslant8\,\overline s\,\operatorname{Var}(\sqrt s)\,.
 \end{split}
\]
If \(\overline s\leqslant2\), then \(\operatorname{Var}(\sqrt s)\geqslant v_s/(4\,(C_4+3))\),
and the first assertion applies. If \(\overline s>2\), reverse Minkowski's inequality gives
\[
 W_2(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt s G_d),\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))
 \geqslant\sqrt d\,(\sqrt{\overline s}-1)
 \geqslant(\sqrt2-1)\sqrt d
 \geqslant(\sqrt2-1)\min\{d v_s,\sqrt{d v_s}\}\,,
\]
since \(v_s\leqslant1\).
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- proposition-12: body_md -->
\label{prop:gaussian-radial-lower}
For every \(d\geqslant1\) and \(0<h<1\), RLMC has a unique invariant
distribution with finite second moment. For \(0<h\leqslant h_0\),
\begin{equation}
    W_2(\widehat\pi_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}},\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))
    \geqslant c\min\{dh^3,\sqrt{dh^3}\}\,.
    \label{eq:gaussian-radial-lower}
\end{equation}
In particular, if $dh^3\leqslant1$, then the lower bound is $cdh^3$.

More precisely, there is a random variable $s_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}} \ge 0$, independent of
$G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)$, such that
\begin{equation}
    \widehat\pi_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}}=\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt{s_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}}}\,G_d)\,,
    \label{eq:gaussian-scale-mixture}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{align}\label{eq:gaussian-scale-mean}
    \overline s_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}}\coloneqq\mathbb{E} s_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}}
    &=\frac{2-2h+h^2}{2-2h+h^2-h^3/3}
      =1+\frac{h^3}{6}+O(h^4)\,, \qquad
    \operatorname{Var}(s_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}})
    =\frac{h^3}{12}+O(h^4)\,.
\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-12 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
In this proof, write \(s_h=s_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}}\),
\(m=m_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}}\), and \(q=q_h^{\mathrm{RLMC}}\), omitting
algorithm labels from the local coefficient calculations.
Conditionally on \(\Theta\sim\mathsf{Unif}[0,1]\), splitting the
Brownian increment at \(\Theta h\) in \eqref{eq:rm-step} gives
\[
 X^+=m(\Theta)\,X+\sqrt{q(\Theta)}\,G_d\,,\qquad
 m(u)\coloneqq1-h+uh^2\,,\qquad q(u)\coloneqq2h\,\{(1-h)^2u+1-u\}\,,
\]
with \(G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\) independent of \((X,\Theta)\).
Since \(0\leqslant m(u)\leqslant1-h+h^2<1\),
\Cref{lem:scalar-linear-mixture} gives existence, uniqueness, and
\eqref{eq:gaussian-scale-mixture}. Its scalar mean formula gives
\[
 \overline s_h=\frac{\mathbb{E} q(\Theta)}{1-\mathbb{E} m(\Theta)^2}
     =\frac{2-2h+h^2}{2-2h+h^2-h^3/3}
     =1+\frac{h^3}{6}+O(h^4)\,.
\]
For the centered covariance forcing, the identity $m(u)^2+q(u)-1=h^2\,(1-2u)+h^4u^2$
implies
\[
 \mathfrak d\coloneqq m(\Theta)^2\,\overline s_h+q(\Theta)-\overline s_h=h^2\,(1-2\Theta)+O(h^3)\,,\qquad
 1-\mathbb{E} m(\Theta)^{2j}=2jh+O(h^2)\quad(j=2,4)\,.
\]
Equation \eqref{eq:linear-mixture-scalar-moments} gives
\[
 \operatorname{Var}(s_h)
   =\frac{h^4/3+O(h^5)}{4h+O(h^2)}=\frac{h^3}{12}+O(h^4)\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s_h-\overline s_h\rvert^4\leqslant\frac{Ch^8}{h^2}=Ch^6\,.
\]
Equation \eqref{eq:mixture-variance-transfer} proves
\eqref{eq:gaussian-radial-lower}.
For \(V=\lambda\,\lVert \cdot\rVert^2/2\), scaling replaces
\(h\) by \(\lambda h\) and \(W_2\) by \(\sqrt\lambda\,W_2\).
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- proposition-13: body_md -->
\label{prop:plmc-gaussian-lower}
For every \(d\geqslant1\), integer \(k\geqslant1\), and
\(0<h\leqslant h_0\), PLMC on \(V=\lVert \cdot\rVert^2/2\) has a unique invariant
distribution with finite second moment which satisfies
\begin{equation}
    W_2(\widehat\pi_h^{\mathrm{PLMC},k},\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))
       \geqslant c\min\{dh^3,\sqrt{dh^3}\}
       \qquad(k\geqslant2)\,.
    \label{eq:poisson-gaussian-radial-lower}
\end{equation}
For \(k=1\), Poisson midpoint is ULA and the stronger bound
\begin{equation}
    W_2(\widehat\pi_h^{\mathrm{PLMC},1},\mathsf{N}(0,I_d))
       \geqslant c\sqrt d\,h
    \label{eq:poisson-k-one-lower}
\end{equation}
holds. The constants are uniform in \(k\).
<!-- end proposition-13 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
For this proof, write
\(m_{\mathsf B}=m_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PLMC}}(\mathsf B)\),
\(q_{\mathsf B}=q_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PLMC}}(\mathsf B)\), and
\(s_{h,k}=s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PLMC}}\); the algorithm and fixed parameters
are omitted from the local coefficients.
For the Bernoulli array \(\mathsf B\) in \eqref{eq:poisson-step}, put
\[
 N_{\mathsf B}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathsf B_i\,,\qquad
 U_{\mathsf B}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac ik\,\mathsf B_i\,,\qquad
 R\coloneqq\sum_{i,j=1}^{k-1}\min\{i/k,j/k\}\,\mathsf B_i\mathsf B_j\,.
\]
Conditional on \(\mathsf B\), the quadratic update is
\begin{equation*}
 X^+=m_{\mathsf B}X+\sqrt{q_{\mathsf B}}G_d\,,\qquad
 m_{\mathsf B}\coloneqq1-h+h^2U_{\mathsf B}\,,\qquad
 q_{\mathsf B}\coloneqq2h-4h^2U_{\mathsf B}+2h^3R\,.
\end{equation*}
Here \(G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\) is independent of \((X,\mathsf B)\),
and \(q_{\mathsf B}\geqslant0\) is the coordinate variance of
\(\sqrt2\,(B_h-h\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathsf B_iB_{hi/k})\).
For \(t\geqslant0\),
\begin{equation}
 \mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{tN_{\mathsf B}}
 =\Bigl(1+\frac{\mathrm{e}^t-1}{k}\Bigr)^{k-1}
 \leqslant\exp(\mathrm{e}^t-1)\,,\qquad U_{\mathsf B}\leqslant N_{\mathsf B}\,,\quad
 R\leqslant N_{\mathsf B}^2\,.
 \label{eq:bernoulli-count-moments}
\end{equation}
Consequently all fixed moments below are uniform in \(k\), and
\[
 \mathbb{E} m_{\mathsf B}^{2j}=1-2jh+O(h^2)\quad(1\leqslant j\leqslant4)\,.
\]
\Cref{lem:scalar-linear-mixture} gives the invariant distribution
\(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt{s_{h,k}}G_d)\). Since
\[
 m_{\mathsf B}^2+q_{\mathsf B}-1
 =h^2\,(1-2U_{\mathsf B})+2h^3\,(R-U_{\mathsf B})+h^4U_{\mathsf B}^2\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E} U_{\mathsf B}=\frac{k-1}{2k}\,,
\]
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-scalar-moments} gives
\[
 \overline s_{h,k}\coloneqq\mathbb{E} s_{h,k}\,,\qquad \overline s_{h,k}-1
 =\frac{\mathbb{E}[m_{\mathsf B}^2+q_{\mathsf B}-1]}{1-\mathbb{E} m_{\mathsf B}^2}
 =O(h/k+h^2)\,.
\]
For \(k\geqslant2\), the centered forcing satisfies
\[
 \mathfrak d\coloneqq m_{\mathsf B}^2\,\overline s_{h,k}+q_{\mathsf B}-\overline s_{h,k}
       =-2h^2\,(U_{\mathsf B}-\mathbb{E} U_{\mathsf B})+O_{L^4}(h^3)\,.
\]
Indeed, centering the defect removes its mean, and
\((\overline s_{h,k}-1)(m_{\mathsf B}^2-\mathbb{E} m_{\mathsf B}^2)=O_{L^4}(h^3)\).
Independence of the indicators gives
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Var}(U_{\mathsf B})
 =\frac1k\,\Bigl(1-\frac1k\Bigr)\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{i^2}{k^2}
 =\frac{(k-1)^2\,(2k-1)}{6k^3}\in[1/16,1/3)\,.
 \label{eq:poisson-quadrature-variance}
\end{equation}
Equation \eqref{eq:linear-mixture-scalar-moments} now gives
\[
 \operatorname{Var}(s_{h,k})
 =h^3\operatorname{Var}(U_{\mathsf B})+O(h^4)\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s_{h,k}-\overline s_{h,k}\rvert^4\leqslant Ch^6\,.
\]
\Cref{lem:gaussian-scale-perturbation} proves
\eqref{eq:poisson-gaussian-radial-lower} uniformly in \(k\geqslant2\).

For \(k=1\), \(m_{\mathsf B}=1-h\), \(q_{\mathsf B}=2h\), and
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-scalar-moments} gives the deterministic variance
\(s_{h,1}=(1-h/2)^{-1}\). Thus
\(W_2=\sqrt d\,((1-h/2)^{-1/2}-1)\geqslant c\sqrt d\,h\),
proving \eqref{eq:poisson-k-one-lower}.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
Apply \Cref{prop:gaussian-radial-lower,prop:plmc-gaussian-lower} in
dimension \(d\). They give existence, uniqueness, finite second moments,
and \eqref{eq:overdamped-common-RLMC}--\eqref{eq:overdamped-common-ULA},
with constants uniform in \(k\). A fixed sufficiently small stationary
accuracy forces \(h\lesssim d^{-1/3}\) for RLMC and PLMC with
\(k\geqslant2\), and \(h\lesssim d^{-1/2}\) for PLMC with \(k=1\).
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{lem:conditional-covariance-fluctuations}
For every \(d\geqslant1\) and \(0<h\leqslant h_0\), there is a non-negative random variable
\(s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}\), independent of \(G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\), such that
\begin{equation}
    (\bm\pi_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}})_x
    =\operatorname{Law}\bigl(\sqrt{s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}}\,G_d\bigr)\,.
    \label{eq:Gaussian-scale-mixture}
\end{equation}
It satisfies
\[
    \operatorname{Var}(s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}})
       =\frac{7h^3}{96}+O(h^4)\,, \qquad
    \mathbb{E}\lvert s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}-\mathbb{E} s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}\rvert^4\leqslant Ch^6\,.
\]
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
By \Cref{lem:random-linear-mixture}, \(s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}\coloneqq(S_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}})_{11}\)
gives \eqref{eq:Gaussian-scale-mixture}. Put
\[
 \Psi_{h,\theta}^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(S)\coloneqq M_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)S M_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)^\top+Q_h(\theta)\,.
\]
Uniformly in \(\theta\in[0,1]\), expansion of
\eqref{eq:M-RULMC} and \eqref{eq:noise-kernel-lower} gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 M_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)&=I_2+hA+
 h^2\,\begin{bmatrix}\theta-1&-1\\2-2\theta&2-\theta\end{bmatrix}+O(h^3)\,,\\
 Q_h(\theta)&=\begin{bmatrix}O(h^3)&2h^2+O(h^3)\\
                2h^2+O(h^3)&4h-8h^2+O(h^3)\end{bmatrix}\,.
 \end{aligned}
\]
Consequently,
\[
 \Psi_{h,\theta}^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(I_2)-I_2
 =h^2\,(2\theta-1)\,\begin{bmatrix}1&-1\\-1&-1\end{bmatrix}+O(h^3)\,.
\]
Since \(\mathbb{E}(2\Theta-1)=0\), the mean equation
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-fixed-point} and the contraction bound give
\[
 \lVert \overline S_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}-I_2\rVert
 \leqslant\frac Ch\,\lVert \mathbb{E}\Psi_{h,\Theta}^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(I_2)-I_2\rVert
 \leqslant Ch^2\,.
\]
Use \(\operatorname{vech}(S)=(S_{11},S_{12},S_{22})^\top\). Define
\(L_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)\) as the \(3\times3\) matrix whose action is
specified, for every symmetric \(2\times2\) matrix \(T\), by
\[
 L_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)\operatorname{vech}(T)
 =\operatorname{vech}\bigl(M_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)T M_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)^\top\bigr)\,.
\]
The identity \(\operatorname{vech}(AS+SA^\top)=F\operatorname{vech}(S)\) gives the expansion
\begin{equation}
 L_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)=I_3+hF+O(h^2)\,,\qquad
 F\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}0&2&0\\-1&-2&1\\0&-2&-4\end{bmatrix}\,.
 \label{eq:covariance-generator}
\end{equation}
Since \(\overline S_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}=I_2+O(h^2)\), the centered forcing
\(\mathfrak d_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)\coloneqq \operatorname{vech}(\Psi_{h,\theta}^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\overline S_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}})
-\overline S_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}})\) satisfies
\[
 \mathfrak d_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}(\theta)=h^2\,(2\theta-1)\,v+O(h^3)\,,\qquad
 v\coloneqq(1,-1,-1)^\top\,.
\]
The eigenvalues of \(F\) are all \(-2\), and
\begin{equation}
 e_1^\top\mathrm{e}^{tF}
 =\mathrm{e}^{-2t}\,\begin{bmatrix}(1+t)^2&2t\,(1+t)&t^2\end{bmatrix}\,.
 \label{eq:covariance-position-row}
\end{equation}
Thus, \eqref{eq:linear-mixture-asymptotic-moments} and
\(\mathbb{E}(2\Theta-1)^2=1/3\) give
 \begin{align*}
 \operatorname{Var}(s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}})
 &=\frac{h^3}{3}\,\int_0^\infty(1-2t^2)^2\mathrm{e}^{-4t}\,\mathrm d t+O(h^4)
   =\frac{7h^3}{96}+O(h^4)\,,\\
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}-\mathbb{E} s_h^{\mathrm{RULMC}}\rvert^4&\leqslant Ch^6\,. \qedhere
 \end{align*}
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
Apply \eqref{eq:mixture-variance-transfer} to
\Cref{lem:conditional-covariance-fluctuations}.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- lemma-23: body_md -->
\label{lem:pulmc-covariance-fluctuations}
For every \(d\geqslant1\), integer \(k\geqslant1\), and
\(0<h\leqslant h_0\), PULMC has a unique invariant distribution
\(\bm\pi_h^{\mathrm{PULMC},k}\in\mathcal P_2\), whose position marginal is
\(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt{s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}}G_d)\), with
\(s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}\geqslant0\) independent of
\(G_d\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\). For \(k\geqslant2\),
\[
 \begin{split}
 \operatorname{Var}(s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}})
 &=\frac{(k-1)^2\,(2k-1)}{64k^3}\,h^3+O(h^4)\,, \qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}-\mathbb{E} s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}\rvert^4
\leqslant Ch^6\,.
 \end{split}
\]
For \(k=1\), the scale is deterministic and equals \(1+h/4+O(h^2)\).
All constants and remainders are uniform in \(k\).
<!-- end lemma-23 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
In this proof, write
\(M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)=M_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}(\mathsf B)\) and
\(Q_{h,k}(\mathsf B)=Q_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}(\mathsf B)\), suppressing the
algorithm label in the local coefficients and covariance map.
Put \(\ell_i\coloneqq kE_{h-t_{i+1}}g_\Delta\) and
\(N_{\mathsf B}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathsf B_i\). Substitution of the
predictor in \eqref{eq:Poisson-quadratic-macrostep} gives the conditional
state matrix and additive noise
\begin{equation*}
 \begin{split}
 M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)
 &\coloneqq E_h-g_he_1^\top+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathsf B_i\ell_i\,
                  \begin{bmatrix}c_{t_i}&-a_{t_i}\end{bmatrix}\,, \qquad
 \text{noise}=\eta_h-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathsf B_i\ell_i(\eta_{t_i})_1\,.
 \end{split}
\end{equation*}
Let \(Q_{h,k}(\mathsf B)\) be this noise's conditional covariance, and
\(Q_h^0\coloneqq\operatorname{Cov}(\eta_h)\).
Writing \(E_{22}\coloneqq e_2e_2^\top\), the coefficients in
\eqref{eq:quadratic-flow-coefficients} satisfy, uniformly in \(i,k\),
\[
 \lVert \ell_i\rVert\leqslant Ch\,,\qquad
 (\ell_i)_2=h+O(h^2)\,,\qquad
 \ell_i\,\begin{bmatrix}c_{t_i}&-a_{t_i}\end{bmatrix}
       =-h^2\,\frac ik\, E_{22}+O(h^3)\,.
\]
Also, \eqref{eq:quadratic-flow-noise} gives
\(\lVert \eta_h\rVert_{L^2}\leqslant C\sqrt h\) and
\(\lVert (\eta_{t_i})_1\rVert_{L^2}\leqslant Ch^{3/2}\). Hence
\[
 \begin{split}
 \lVert Q_{h,k}(\mathsf B)-Q_h^0\rVert
   &\leqslant C\,(N_{\mathsf B}h^3+N_{\mathsf B}^2h^5)\,,\\
 \lVert M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)\rVert_{H_{\mathrm{hyp}}}
   &\leqslant1-ch+Ch^2\,(1+N_{\mathsf B})\,,\qquad
 \operatorname{tr}Q_{h,k}(\mathsf B)
   \leqslant Ch\,(1+N_{\mathsf B})^2\,.
 \end{split}
\]
The exponential moment bound \eqref{eq:bernoulli-count-moments} implies
\(\mathbb{E}\lVert M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)\rVert_{H_{\mathrm{hyp}}}^8\leqslant1-ch\), uniformly in
\(k\), after reducing \(h_0\). Thus \Cref{lem:random-linear-mixture}
gives existence, uniqueness, and the position marginal
\(\operatorname{Law}(\sqrt{s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}}G_d)\), where
\(s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}\coloneqq(S_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}})_{11}\).

For \(k=1\), the correction sum vanishes. Expansion of
\eqref{eq:quadratic-flow-coefficients}--\eqref{eq:quadratic-flow-noise}
gives
\[
 (E_h-g_he_1^\top)(E_h-g_he_1^\top)^\top+Q_h^0-I_2
 =h^2E_{22}+O(h^3)\,.
\]
The mean equation \eqref{eq:linear-mixture-fixed-point} and
\(AI_2/4+I_2 A^\top/4=-E_{22}\) imply
\(S_{h,1}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}=I_2+hI_2/4+O(h^2)\).
Indeed, the covariance operator divided by \(h\) converges to
\(T\mapsto-AT-TA^\top\), whose inverse is
\(Q\mapsto\int_0^\infty\mathrm{e}^{tA}Q\mathrm{e}^{tA^\top}\,\mathrm d t\).

For \(k\geqslant2\), write
\(\Psi_{h,k,\mathsf B}(S)\coloneqq M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)S M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)^\top
+Q_{h,k}(\mathsf B)\).
The preceding coefficient bounds give, in every fixed \(L^q\) norm
over \(\mathsf B\),
\[
 \begin{split}
 M_{h,k}-\mathbb{E} M_{h,k}
 &=-h^2\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\Bigl(\mathsf B_i-\frac1k\Bigr)
                      \frac ik\, E_{22}+O_{L^q}(h^3)\,,\\
 Q_{h,k}-\mathbb{E} Q_{h,k}&=O_{L^q}(h^3)\,,\qquad
 M_{h,k}=I_2+hA+O_{L^q}(h^2)\,.
 \end{split}
\]
In particular,
\[
 \begin{split}
 \Psi_{h,k,\mathsf B}(I_2)-\mathbb{E}\Psi_{h,k,\mathsf B}(I_2)
 &=h^2\xi_k(\mathsf B)E_{22}+O_{L^4}(h^3)\,, \qquad
 \xi_k(\mathsf B)\coloneqq-2\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}
             \Bigl(\mathsf B_i-\frac1k\Bigr)\,\frac ik\,.
 \end{split}
\]
The mean defect is \(O(h^2)\). Since
\(\mathbb{E}\lVert M_{h,k}\rVert_{H_{\mathrm{hyp}}}^2\leqslant1-ch\), the mean equation in
\eqref{eq:linear-mixture-fixed-point} gives
\(\overline S\coloneqq\mathbb{E} S_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}=I_2+O(h)\).
Centering at \(\overline S\) instead of \(I_2\) adds only
\[
 M_{h,k}\,(\overline S-I_2)M_{h,k}^\top
 -\mathbb{E}[M_{h,k}\,(\overline S-I_2)M_{h,k}^\top]=O_{L^4}(h^3)\,.
\]
In the coordinates \(\operatorname{vech}(S)=(S_{11},S_{12},S_{22})^\top\), define
\(L_{h,k}\) as the random \(3\times3\) matrix whose action on every
symmetric \(2\times2\) matrix \(T\) is specified by $L_{h,k}\operatorname{vech}(T)
 =\operatorname{vech}(M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)T M_{h,k}(\mathsf B)^\top)$.
Define the centered covariance forcing $\mathfrak d_{h,k}\coloneqq\operatorname{vech}(\Psi_{h,k,\mathsf B}(\overline S)-\overline S)$.
Both depend on the Bernoulli array \(\mathsf B\). The coefficient bounds give
\[
 L_{h,k}=I_3+hF+O_{L^4}(h^2)\,,\qquad
 \mathfrak d_{h,k}=h^2\xi_k e_3+O_{L^4}(h^3)\,,
\]
with \(F\) from \eqref{eq:covariance-generator}. By
\eqref{eq:poisson-quadrature-variance},
\[
 \sigma_k^2\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\xi_k^2
 =\frac{2\,(k-1)^2\,(2k-1)}{3k^3}\,.
\]
Equations \eqref{eq:linear-mixture-asymptotic-moments} and
\eqref{eq:covariance-position-row} now give
\begin{align*}
 \operatorname{Var}(s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}})
 &=h^3\sigma_k^2\int_0^\infty t^4\mathrm{e}^{-4t}\,\mathrm d t+O(h^4)
   =\vartheta_kh^3+O(h^4)\,,\\
 \vartheta_k&\coloneqq\frac{(k-1)^2(2k-1)}{64k^3}\in[3/512,1/32)\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}-\mathbb{E} s_{h,k}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}\rvert^4
       \leqslant Ch^6\,. \qedhere
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
For \(k\geqslant2\), apply \Cref{lem:gaussian-scale-perturbation} to
\Cref{lem:pulmc-covariance-fluctuations}, using
\((k-1)^2\,(2k-1)/(64k^3)\geqslant3/512\).
For \(k=1\), the Gaussian formula gives
\(W_2=\sqrt d\,\lvert\sqrt{s_{h,1}^{\mathrm{PULMC}}}-1\rvert
\geqslant c\sqrt d\,h\).
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- lemma-24: body_md -->
\label{lem:finite-time-underdamped-covariance}
Start a Gaussian coordinate pair from \(\mathsf{N}(0,I_2)\), independently
of the future updates, and let \(S_n\) be its conditional covariance
after \(n\) steps, given the quadrature variables. Put \(s_n\coloneqq(S_n)_{11}\).
These are the covariances of the method under consideration;
we suppress its algorithm label and the fixed parameters \(h\) and \(k\)
in this lemma.
For RULMC and PULMC with \(k\geqslant2\),
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathbb{E} S_n&=I_2+O(h)\,,\qquad
 \operatorname{Var}(s_n)
 =h^3\sigma^2\int_0^{nh}
       (e_1^\top\mathrm{e}^{tF}v)^2\,\mathrm d t+O(h^4)\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\lvert s_n-\mathbb{E} s_n\rvert^4\leqslant Ch^6\,,
 \end{split}
\]
where \(F\) is defined in \eqref{eq:covariance-generator}, and we have:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c}
 \text{Method}&v&\sigma^2\\ \hline
 \mathrm{RULMC}&(1,-1,-1)^\top&1/3\\
 \mathrm{PULMC}\,,\ k\geqslant2&e_3&2\,(k-1)^2(2k-1)/(3k^3)
 \end{array}
\]
For PULMC with \(k=1\), \(S_n\) is deterministic and
\[
 s_n-1=h\int_0^{nh}t^2\mathrm{e}^{-2t}\,\mathrm d t+O(h^2)\,.
\]
The constants and remainders are uniform over \(n\geqslant0\),
\(k\), and \(0<h\leqslant h_0\).
<!-- end lemma-24 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
Write \(\Psi_j(S)\coloneqq M_jSM_j^\top+Q_j\) for the fresh covariance
map at step \(j\), so \(S_0=I_2\) and \(S_{j+1}=\Psi_j(S_j)\).
The coefficient calculations in
\Cref{lem:conditional-covariance-fluctuations,lem:pulmc-covariance-fluctuations}
give
\[
 \mathbb{E}\lVert M_j\rVert_{H_{\mathrm{hyp}}}^8\leqslant1-ch\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\Psi_j(I_2)-I_2=O(h^2)\,.
\]
Thus the operator \(\mathcal T(T)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[M_jTM_j^\top]\) has
\(\lVert \mathcal T^j\rVert\leqslant C\mathrm{e}^{-cjh}\) in fixed matrix coordinates.
With \(\overline S_j\coloneqq\mathbb{E} S_j\), iteration gives
\[
 \overline S_n-I_2
 =\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathcal T^j
       \bigl(\mathbb{E}\Psi_0(I_2)-I_2\bigr)=O(h)
\]
uniformly in \(n\).

Use the coordinates \(\operatorname{vech}(T)=(T_{11},T_{12},T_{22})^\top\), and set
\[
 R_j\coloneqq \operatorname{vech}(S_j-\overline S_j)\,,\qquad
 L_j\operatorname{vech}(T)= \operatorname{vech}(M_jTM_j^\top)\,,\qquad
 \mathfrak d_j\coloneqq \operatorname{vech}\bigl(\Psi_j(\overline S_j)-\overline S_{j+1}\bigr)\,.
\]
Then, \(R_{j+1}=L_jR_j+\mathfrak d_j\), with \(\mathbb{E} R_j=\mathbb{E}\mathfrak d_j=0\),
and \(R_j\) is independent of \((L_j,\mathfrak d_j)\).
The expansions in
\eqref{eq:M-RULMC} and
\eqref{eq:Poisson-quadratic-macrostep} give
\(M_j-\mathbb{E} M_j=O_{L^8}(h^2)\). Together with
\(\overline S_j-I_2=O(h)\), this shows that centering the
covariance map at \(\overline S_j\) instead of \(I_2\) changes its
centered forcing by only \(O_{L^4}(h^3)\). The centered defects
computed in the two cited lemmas therefore give, uniformly in \(j\),
\[
 L_j=I_3+hF+O_{L^4}(h^2)\,,\qquad
 \mathfrak d_j=h^2\xi_jv+O_{L^4}(h^3)\,,\qquad
 \mathbb{E}\xi_j=0\,,\quad \mathbb{E}\xi_j^2=\sigma^2\,,\quad
 \lVert \xi_j\rVert_{L^4}\leqslant C\,.
\]
Here \(\xi_j\coloneqq2\Theta_j-1\) for RULMC and
\(\xi_j\coloneqq-2\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(\mathsf B_{j,i}-1/k)\,i/k\) for PULMC.

Let \(\Gamma_j\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[R_jR_j^\top]\) and
\(\mathcal C(T)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[L_jTL_j^\top]\).
Independence and centering give
\[
 \Gamma_{j+1}=\mathcal C(\Gamma_j)
                   +h^4\sigma^2vv^\top+O(h^5)\,,\qquad \Gamma_0=0\,.
\]
The contraction estimate implies
\(\lVert \mathcal C^j\rVert\leqslant C\mathrm{e}^{-cjh}\), and
\(\mathcal C(T)=T+h\,(FT+TF^\top)+O(h^2)\,\lVert T\rVert\).
The matrices
\[
 \widetilde\Gamma_j\coloneqq h^3\sigma^2\int_0^{jh}
          \mathrm{e}^{tF}vv^\top\mathrm{e}^{tF^\top}\,\mathrm d t
\]
satisfy the same recursion with a residual \(O(h^5)\): stability of
\(F\) gives \(\widetilde\Gamma_j=O(h^3)\) uniformly, so the
\(O(h^2)\) difference between \(\mathcal C\) and the exact covariance
flow contributes only \(O(h^5)\).
Summing these residuals using contraction gives
\(\Gamma_j=\widetilde\Gamma_j+O(h^4)\) uniformly in \(j\), proving the
variance formula.

For the fourth moment, the finite expansion
\[
 R_n=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}L_{n-1}\cdots L_{j+1}\mathfrak d_j
\]
has centered martingale differences when the coefficient pairs are
revealed in the order \(n-1,n-2,\ldots,0\). The fourth moment
argument in \Cref{lem:random-linear-mixture}, applied to this finite
sum, gives
\[
 \lVert R_n\rVert_{L^4}^2
 \leqslant C\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}
       \mathrm{e}^{-c(n-1-j)h}\,\lVert \mathfrak d_j\rVert_{L^4}^2
 \leqslant Ch^3\,.
\]

For PULMC with \(k=1\), the deterministic covariance defect computed
in \Cref{lem:pulmc-covariance-fluctuations} is
\(\Psi_j(I_2)-I_2=h^2e_2e_2^\top+O(h^3)\), and
\(M_j=I_2+hA+O(h^2)\).
The same comparison of recursions, now for \(S_n-I_2\), gives
\[
 S_n-I_2
 =h\int_0^{nh}\mathrm{e}^{tA}e_2e_2^\top\mathrm{e}^{tA^\top}\,\mathrm d t
       +O(h^2)
\]
uniformly in \(n\). Since \(e_1^\top\mathrm{e}^{tA}e_2=t\mathrm{e}^{-t}\), its
position entry is the asserted formula.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- proof-41: body_md -->
We first prove the bounds at bounded condition number.
For each overdamped method, let \(\mu_h\) be the numerical invariant
distribution of its first \(d-1\) coordinates. Use the position starts
\[
 \operatorname{Law}(X_0)=
 \begin{cases}
  \mu_h\otimes\delta_1\,,&\text{Heun, RLMC, and PLMC}\,,\\
  \delta_{e_d}\,,&\text{exponential midpoint}\,,\\
  \mathsf{N}(e_d,I_d)\,,&\text{RULMC and PULMC}\,.
 \end{cases}
\]
For every underdamped method take \(P_0\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_d)\)
independently of \(X_0\), and make every initialization independent of
future innovations. All the targets are centered, and \(\alpha\asymp1\), so
normalized and unnormalized accuracy differ only by universal constants.

\emph{A common lower bound on elapsed time.}
For any algorithm, let \(\overline M_h\) denote the expected
one-step coefficient of the final coordinate: in the overdamped case,
the mean of the scalar coefficient multiplying the current \(d\)-th
position; in the underdamped case, the mean of the \(2\times2\) state
matrix acting on the \(d\)th position--momentum pair. For Heun and RLMC it is \(1-h+h^2/2\);
for PLMC it is \(1-h+(k-1)h^2/(2k)\). The underdamped formulas
\eqref{eq:exponential-midpoint-matrix}, \eqref{eq:M-RULMC},
and \eqref{eq:Poisson-quadratic-macrostep}, together
with \eqref{eq:bernoulli-count-moments}, give
\(\lVert \overline M_h-I\rVert\leqslant Ch\), uniformly in \(k\).
The same bound holds for the overdamped case. Independence of fresh
coefficients from the current state therefore gives, for the difference of the mean in the $d$-th position coordinate from the initial mean,
\[
 \lvert\mathbb{E} X_{N,d}-1\rvert
 \leqslant\lVert \overline M_h^N-I\rVert
 \leqslant(1+Ch)^N-1\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{CNh}-1\,.
\]
Fix a universal \(\tau>0\) so that this is at most \(1/2\) when
\(Nh\leqslant\tau\). Since $W_2$ is lower bounded by the difference
of means, accuracy \(\varepsilon\leqslant\varepsilon_0\) for a small
enough universal \(\varepsilon_0\) requires \(Nh>\tau\).

\emph{Error bounds after that elapsed time.}
For the overdamped methods, the first \(d-1\) block of coordinates retain the stationary
marginal distribution \(\mu_h\) at every iteration. Indeed,
the updates of this block do not involve the final coordinate; after
averaging over the quadrature variables, they form the same method in
dimension \(d-1\), for which \(\mu_h\) is invariant. Conditional on the
quadrature variables the law of the block does change; only its average
over these variables is stationary.
Projection onto this block, the scalar Heun bound from the proof of
\Cref{prop:heun-oscillatory-lower} summed over its \(d-1\geqslant d/2\)
oscillatory coordinates, and
\Cref{prop:gaussian-radial-lower,prop:plmc-gaussian-lower}
in dimension \(d-1\geqslant d/2\)
give errors at least \(c\sqrt d\,h^{3/2}\) for Heun,
\(c\min\{dh^3,\sqrt{dh^3}\}\) for RLMC and PLMC with \(k\geqslant2\),
and \(c\sqrt d\,h\) for PLMC with \(k=1\).

For exponential midpoint, let \(C_{N,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\) be the covariance of each
position--momentum pair after \(N\) steps, so that
\(C_{0,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}=\operatorname{diag}(0,1)\). At the fixed step size \(h\), denote
its invariant covariance by
\(C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\coloneqq C_{h,1}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\). The invariant covariance expansion in
the proof of \Cref{prop:exponential-midpoint-Gaussian-lower} gives
\[
 (C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-C_{0,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{11}=1+O(h^2)\,,\qquad
 \det(C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-C_{0,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})=h^2/12+O(h^3)\,.
\]
Thus \(C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-C_{0,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\succeq0\) for sufficiently small \(h\), and
\eqref{eq:exponential-midpoint-Lyapunov} yields
\[
 C_{N,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}=C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-M_h^N\,(C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}-C_{0,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})(M_h^N)^\top\preceq C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\,.
\]
The position covariance at time $N$ is \(s_{N,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}I_d\), with
\(s_{N,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}\coloneqq(C_{N,h}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{11}\leqslant(C_h^{\mathrm{ExpMid}})_{11}=s_{h,1}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}=1-h^2/24+O(h^3)<1\). The Gaussian Wasserstein
formula consequently gives an error at least
\(\sqrt d\,(1-\sqrt{s_{h,1}^{\mathrm{ExpMid}}})\geqslant c\sqrt d\,h^2\) at every iteration.

For the randomized underdamped methods, the first \(d-1\) positions
are conditionally independent centered Gaussians with common variance
\(s_N\) from \Cref{lem:finite-time-underdamped-covariance}. Every
vector \(v\) in that lemma satisfies $\int_0^\tau(e_1^\top\mathrm{e}^{tF}v)^2\,\mathrm d t>0$
by \eqref{eq:covariance-position-row}, and \(\sigma^2\) is uniformly
bounded below. The universal \(\tau\) was fixed small by the
mean calculation for the final coordinate. Since the integrand is non-negative, for \(Nh>\tau\) the
integral in \Cref{lem:finite-time-underdamped-covariance} is at least
its value over \([0,\tau]\), which is positive and depends only on
\(\tau\). It is also at most the integral over \((0,\infty)\), which
is finite because \(\mathrm{e}^{tF}\) decays exponentially. After shrinking the
universal \(h_0\) so that the \(O(h^4)\) remainder is at most half the
leading term, the lemma gives
\(\operatorname{Var}(s_N)\asymp h^3\) and
\(\mathbb{E}\lvert s_N-\mathbb{E} s_N\rvert^4\lesssim h^6\) whenever \(Nh>\tau\).
Projection and \Cref{lem:gaussian-scale-perturbation}
in dimension \(d-1\geqslant d/2\) give an error at
least \(c\min\{dh^3,\sqrt{dh^3}\}\). For PULMC with \(k=1\), the
lemma instead gives \(s_N-1\geqslant ch\) for \(Nh>\tau\), by
the same comparison with the integral over \([0,\tau]\), and the Gaussian
Wasserstein formula gives \(c\sqrt d\,h\).

The preceding errors impose the step restrictions
\[
 h\lesssim
 \begin{cases}
  \varepsilon^{2/3}d^{-1/3}\,,&\text{Heun}\,,\\
  \varepsilon^{1/2}d^{-1/4}\,,&\text{exponential midpoint}\,,\\
  \varepsilon^{1/3}d^{-1/3}\,,&\text{randomized methods, excluding }k=1\,,\\
  \varepsilon d^{-1/2}\,,&\text{PLMC/PULMC with }k=1\,.
 \end{cases}
\]
Combining each restriction with \(N>\tau/h\) proves all the asserted
iteration bounds, uniformly in \(k\).

\emph{Initialization energy.}
For Heun, each scalar update differs from its quadratic update (\(a_\star=0\)) by at
most \(Ch^{3/2}\), because
\(V'(x)-x=-a_\star\sqrt h\sin(x/\sqrt h)\) on an oscillatory
coordinate, and we take a step of size $h$. The quadratic coefficient has square at most \(1-ch\)
and noise variance at most \(Ch\). Young's inequality absorbs the oscillatory
perturbation into half the contraction, giving
\[
 \mathbb{E}[(X_i^+)^2]\leqslant(1-ch)\,\mathbb{E} X_i^2+Ch\,.
\]
Thus each invariant coordinate has second moment at most \(C\);
the bounded oscillatory potential contributes at most \(Cdh\).
For RLMC and PLMC, the invariant second moments are uniformly bounded
by \eqref{eq:gaussian-scale-mean} and the mean calculation in
\Cref{prop:plmc-gaussian-lower}. Hence all overdamped starts have
\(\mathbb{E}[V(X_0)-V(x_\star)]\leqslant Cd\). The two underdamped starts
have energy gaps \(1/2\) and \((d+1)/2\), respectively, and the
randomized position start has \(W_2(\operatorname{Law}(X_0),\pi)=1\).
Finally, strong convexity gives
\(\alpha\,\mathbb{E}\lVert X_0-x_\star\rVert^2\leqslant2\,\mathbb{E}[V(X_0)-V(x_\star)]\),
while integration by parts gives
\(\alpha\,\mathbb{E}_\pi\lVert X-x_\star\rVert^2\leqslant d\). The triangle inequality through \(\delta_{x_\star}\) therefore yields \(\alphaW_2^2(\nu_0,\pi)\leqslant Cd\).

The final Heun assertion follows from \Cref{thm:et-linear-kappa-lower},
using \eqref{eq:linear-kappa-endpoint-rate} and deducing
the initial Wasserstein bound from its energy bound as above.
<!-- end proof-41 -->
<!-- lemma-25: body_md -->
\label{lem:tb-sectorial-multiplier}
Let \(\mathcal T\) be a closed operator on a Banach space
\(\mathcal X\) that is sectorial of angle \(\omega\in[0,\uppi)\), and let
\(\vartheta\in(\omega,\uppi)\).  Let \(f\colon\Sigma_\vartheta\to\mathbb C\)
be holomorphic, and suppose that there are
\(f(0),f(\infty)\in\mathbb C\) and constants \(C_f,s>0\) such that, for
\(z\in\Sigma_\vartheta\),
\[
    \lvert f(z)-f(0)\rvert\leqslant C_f\,\lvert z\rvert^s
    \quad\text{if }\lvert z\rvert\leqslant1\,,
    \qquad
    \lvert f(z)-f(\infty)\rvert\leqslant C_f\,\lvert z\rvert^{-s}
    \quad\text{if }\lvert z\rvert\geqslant1\,.
\]
For \(h>0\), define
\[
    f(h\mathcal T)
    \coloneqq f(\infty)\,I+(f(0)-f(\infty))\,(I+h\mathcal T)^{-1}
       +g(h\mathcal T)\,,
    \quad
    g(z)\coloneqq f(z)-f(\infty)-\frac{f(0)-f(\infty)}{1+z}\,,
\]
where \(g(h\mathcal T)\) is given by \eqref{eq:tb-contour-calculus} with
\(\eta\coloneqq(\omega+\vartheta)/2\).  Then,
\[
    \sup_{h>0}\,\lVert f(h\mathcal T)\rVert_{\mathcal X\to\mathcal X}
    \leqslant\lvert f(\infty)\rvert
       +\lvert f(0)-f(\infty)\rvert\,M_\eta(\mathcal T)
       +\frac{2C_gM_\eta(\mathcal T)}{\uppi\min\{s,1\}}\,,
    \quad
    C_g\coloneqq C_f+\frac{\lvert f(0)-f(\infty)\rvert}{\cos(\vartheta/2)}\,.
\]
<!-- end lemma-25 -->
<!-- proof-42: body_md -->
First, \(g\) decays at zero and at infinity.  For
\(z\in\Sigma_\vartheta\),
\[
    \lvert1+z\rvert^2
    =(1+\lvert z\rvert)^2-4\,\lvert z\rvert\sin^2\frac{\arg z}2
    \geqslant\cos^2\frac\vartheta2\,(1+\lvert z\rvert)^2\,,
\]
since \(4\,\lvert z\rvert\leqslant(1+\lvert z\rvert)^2\).  If
\(\lvert z\rvert\leqslant1\), write
\(g(z)=f(z)-f(0)+(f(0)-f(\infty))\,z/(1+z)\); if
\(\lvert z\rvert\geqslant1\), write
\(g(z)=f(z)-f(\infty)-(f(0)-f(\infty))/(1+z)\).  With the lower bound on
\(\lvert1+z\rvert\), both cases give
\[
    \lvert g(z)\rvert
    \leqslant C_g\min\{\lvert z\rvert^{s'},\lvert z\rvert^{-s'}\}\,,
    \qquad s'\coloneqq\min\{s,1\}\,,\qquad z\in\Sigma_\vartheta\,.
\]
Next, on the rays \(z=t\mathrm{e}^{\pm\boldsymbol i\eta}\), \(t>0\), of
\(\partial\Sigma_\eta\), scale invariance gives
\(\lVert (z-h\mathcal T)^{-1}\rVert\leqslant M_\eta(h\mathcal T)/t
=M_\eta(\mathcal T)/t\).  The integral in
\eqref{eq:tb-contour-calculus} therefore converges absolutely, and
\[
    \lVert g(h\mathcal T)\rVert
    \leqslant\frac{2}{2\uppi}\int_0^\infty
       C_g\min\{t^{s'},t^{-s'}\}\,\frac{M_\eta(\mathcal T)}t\,\mathrm d t
    =\frac{2C_gM_\eta(\mathcal T)}{\uppi s'}\,.
\]
The decay of \(g\) at zero compensates for the growth of the resolvent
near the origin, and its decay at infinity makes the integral over the
unbounded contour converge.  Finally,
\((I+h\mathcal T)^{-1}=-(-1-h\mathcal T)^{-1}\) is a resolvent at
\(z=-1\), where \(\lvert\arg z\rvert=\uppi\geqslant\eta\), so its norm is
at most \(M_\eta(h\mathcal T)=M_\eta(\mathcal T)\).  The triangle
inequality completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-42 -->
